FAQs

Can Notetaker join anonymously without others knowing or seeing it?

  • No.
  • Notetaker will always join as a guest participant in a meeting.
  • We recommend always obtaining consent and following your local laws regarding recording per the Terms of Service.

Will Notetaker stay in the meeting if it’s silent or there is no audio?

  • No.
  • Notetaker will leave the meeting if silence is detected during a meeting for up to 12 continuous minutes.
  • If a meeting is running beyond its scheduled time, the silence threshold is reduced to 5 minutes.

I have issues with Notetaker joining. Do I need to set up my meeting permissions to allow Notetaker to join?

  • Yes.
  • Notetaker can only join as a guest and cannot sign into any Zoom, Google Meet, or Microsoft Teams account to join the meeting.
  • Check with your IT team to ensure your organization settings allow guest participants to your meetings.

Remove Otter Notetaker from your meeting (Zoom, Google Meet, or Microsoft Teams)

You can remove Notetaker at any time from your Zoom, Google Meet, or Microsoft Teams during a live meeting. This applies to both auto-joined calendar event meetings and meetings you manually add a Notetaker to. #### Type “stop otter” to remove Notetaker All meeting participants can type “stop otter” in the Zoom, Google Meet, or Microsoft Teams meeting chat. All Notetakers will be immediately removed. #### Click “Stop Notetaker” in the Otter conversation Meeting participants can remove Notetaker through the conversation link Meeting participants can open the conversation using the Notetaker link in the chat. To immediately remove Notetaker, click Stop Notetaker, then select Yes, turn it off. #### Stop Notetaker in your Otter conversation Otter users can remove Notetaker from the conversation page” Remove Notetaker at any time from a live meeting, either from the conversation recording page or through the homepage calendar. Click Yes, turn it off to confirm the removal of Notetaker from the meeting. #### Turn off Notetaker auto-join Manage the default auto-join setting for Notetaker To prevent Notetaker from automatically joining your meetings, manage your auto-join settings in Integrations → Meetings tab → Default auto-join settings** and select Meetings I manually select. 👉 Learn more about stopping your Notetaker from automatically joining your meetings. #### Type “stop otter” to remove Notetaker on mobile All meeting participants can type and send “stop otter” in the Zoom, Google Meet, or Microsoft Teams meeting chat to remove Notetaker immediately. #### Click “Stop Notetaker” in the Otter conversation on mobile Meeting participants can stop Notetaker through the conversation link Meeting participants can open the conversation using the Notetaker link in the chat. To immediately remove Notetaker, click Stop Notetaker, then select Yes, turn it off. #### Stop Notetaker in your Otter conversation Otter users can remove Notetaker from the conversation page Remove Notetaker at any time from a live meeting in your Otter account on the conversation page. Click the stop recording button in the conversation. #### Turn off Notetaker auto-join on mobile Manage your Notetaker auto-join settings To prevent Notetaker from automatically joining your meetings, turn off auto-join. 1. Tap on Account → Account Settings. 2. Tap on Meeting settings. 3. Tap on Auto-join meetings, select Manual. **

Note: Previous events that were manually toggled on/off will retain the setting. Review your calendar events in the Calendar tab on mobile after managing this setting if you previously changed individual event settings. #### Remove Notetaker through the meeting controls Users with the appropriate permissions can remove the Notetaker participant directly from a Zoom, Google Meet, or Microsoft Teams meeting. Check with your meeting host or review your meeting platform settings to confirm whether you have permission to remove participants from a meeting. #### (image: zoom.png) Zoom The host or co-host can remove Notetaker as a participant in Zoom during the meeting. #### (image: google meet.png) Google Meet Depending on the meeting permissions, other participants and hosts can remove Notetaker from the meeting. #### (image: microsoft teams.png) Microsoft Teams Depending on the meeting permissions, the organizer and presenter(s) can remove Notetaker from the meeting. #### FAQs #### I removed Notetaker. How do I prevent it from joining future meetings? Review your auto-join settings in Integrations → Meetings tab → Default auto-join settings and select Meetings I manually select. Learn more about Stopping Otter Notetaker from automatically joining your meetings. You can also disconnect your calendar to remove all future events. #### Can any meeting participant stop Notetaker? Yes. Any meeting participant can stop Notetaker. Type “stop otter” in the Zoom, Google Meet, or Microsoft Teams meeting chat to remove Notetaker. #### Do I need an Otter account to remove Notetaker? No. Any meeting participant, including those without an Otter account, can remove Notetaker. Type “stop otter” in the Zoom, Google Meet, or Microsoft Teams meeting chat to remove Notetaker.

Set up Otter Notetaker to join your Zoom meeting

Follow the steps in this article to set up Otter Notetaker to join your Zoom meetings. Following the steps below will ensure Notetaker can join each meeting successfully and cover the compliance requirements set by Zoom. #### Set up Notetaker to join your Zoom meeting Otter offers an easy method to ensure your Zoom meetings allow Notetaker to record meetings you host successfully. To get started, follow the two steps below. - Step 1: Manage your Zoom settings - Step 2: Connect your Zoom account to Otter

Step 1: Manage your Zoom settings
  1. Sign in to your Zoom account.
  2. Under Personal on the left navigation menu, click Settings. Navigate to the Recording tab.
  3. Toggle ON** Record to computer files**.
  4. Checkmark External meeting participants.
  5. Checkmark Auto approve their permission request.
  6. Click Save.
Step 2: Connect your Zoom account to Otter
  1. Sign in to Otter.
  2. Click Integrations on the left navigation menu. Click Add next to Zoom.
  3. Sign in to your Zoom account.
  4. Review the permissions and click Allow to continue.

Notetaker is now connected to your Zoom account. Yours and other users’ Notetakers can now join all Zoom meetings that you host and record automatically. You are all set and connected for Notetaker. ##### FAQs #### The meeting allows participants to join before the host. Will Notetaker be able to record if the host is not present? - Yes. - Otter AI Notetaker can join and record meetings even if the host is not present, as long as the host has connected their Otter account to their Zoom account in advance. - This connection authorizes Otter to automatically join and record; without it, Notetaker may be blocked until the host arrives. - Step-by-step instructions are at the top of this Help Center article, and must be completed by the host: 👉 Set up Otter Notetaker to join your Zoom meeting

Can Notetaker join meetings hosted by Zoom Rooms?

  • No.
  • Meetings hosted by Zoom Rooms are not supported at this time. The Notetaker will not be able to join, as the host (Zoom Rooms) is unable to approve the permissions.
  • Make sure the meeting is scheduled by a host or reassign the host privileges to another participant who has connected their Otter account to Zoom.
  • Step-by-step instructions are at the top of this Help Center article, and must be completed by the host: 👉 Set up Otter Notetaker to join your Zoom meeting

Does the host have to click allow recording each time?

  • No.The host will not need to click allow recording each time if the host has an Otter account and has followed the steps above to connect their Otter account to their Zoom account.

  • The host will need to click Allow Recording each time the Notetaker joins for the first time in the meeting if they do not have an Otter account. Please have the host follow the instructions in our Otter Notetaker is not joining my Zoom meeting article to resolve any errors, if they do not have an Otter account.

If multiple Notetakers join my meeting, does the host have to click Allow Recording for each Notetaker?

  • No.Once you have followed the steps above and connected your Zoom account, both yours and any other Notetaker will be able to join the meetings you host seamlessly.

  • If the host does not have an Otter account, they will need to click Allow Recording on the pop-up each time.

  • If the host did not check Apply these permissions to all future requests in this meeting, then the pop-up will appear for each Notetaker that joins.

  • Each Notetaker that joins the meeting will require the host to allow recording before the Notetaker can transcribe the meeting.

  • If the host checked Apply these permissions to all future requests in this meeting on the pop-up for the first Notetaker, other Notetakers that join do not need the host to allow recording. This setting only applies to the current meeting. (image: apply these permissions to all future requests in this meeting.jpg)

Note: This setting only applies to the current meeting and not future meetings. If the host did not follow the steps above, they will need to click Approve for** **each meeting.

Can participants click Allow Recording instead of the host to allow Notetakers to record the meeting?

  • No.
  • Only the host of the meeting can see the pop-up to allow recording and permit Notetaker to record.
  • As a participant, you only need to accept the default meeting is being recorded disclaimer pop-up by Zoom.

Can the co-host allow recording and permit Notetaker to record?

  • No.
  • Co-host(s) will not see the pop-up and cannot permit Notetaker to record.
  • If the host leaves the meeting, and the co-host becomes the primary host, they will then be able to approve all future pop-ups during that meeting.
  • Co-host(s) will need to accept the default meeting is being recorded disclaimer pop-up by Zoom.

As a co-host, if I become the primary host, can I allow recording and permit Notetaker to record?

  • Yes.
  • If you are the co-host, but become the primary host, any future Notetaker joins will notify you as the host (only within the current meeting) if there are recording permission prompts.

If I am attending a Zoom meeting on my mobile device, will I need to allow Notetaker to record?

  • No.If you are the host and followed the steps above to connect your Otter account to Zoom, all Notetakers will be able to join and transcribe the meeting.

  • If the host is on mobile and does not have an Otter account, they will need to allow Notetaker to record through the pop-up.

Can I leave the “auto-approve their permission request” setting unchecked?

  • Yes.
  • Only the External meeting participants setting is required.
  • You can leave the Auto approve their permission requests setting unchecked.
  • If you are hosting the meeting, you will need to manually approve the Notetaker(s) to join each time if you turn off the setting.
  • Approve Notetaker during the meeting if you turn off that setting.

Stop Otter Notetaker from automatically joining your meetings

Otter Notetaker is an excellent feature that can automatically join your Zoom, Google Meet, and Microsoft Teams meetings via your synced calendar events and provide real-time transcription. There may be situations where you would like to turn off this feature. Toggle off Notetaker’s auto-join setting, and Otter will no longer automatically join your synced calendar events. There are multiple ways to access your Notetaker meeting auto-join settings. #### Homepage AI Notetaker Settings Easily access your Notetaker auto-join settings through the homepage On the Meetings tab, click on AI Notetaker settings and select Meetings I manually select. #### Account Settings AI Notetaker Settings Manage your Notetaker settings in your account settings Click Integrations → Meetings tab → Default auto-join settings** and select Meetings I manually select. ** #### Review your calendar events See a quick overview of which individual events you previously toggled auto-join on for on the homepage calendar. Review your calendar events and verify that Notetaker is toggled off for your upcoming meetings.

Turn off auto-share for conversations & meetings

You have full control over permission sharing for your conversations and meetings. The auto-share setting affects all calendar event recordings. Turning off auto-share will prevent notes from being shared at the start of the meeting. #### Turn off auto-share settings 1. Sign in to Otter.ai. 2. Click the gear settings icon on the upper corner of the homepage calendar. (image: meeting settings.png) 3. Click on the drop-down menu next to Default audience for shared notes. Select Don’t Share-keep my notes private.

Note: Changing this setting will not affect calendar events that you have manually set auto-share for. Review your meetings on the home page calendar to adjust auto-share settings per event.  (image: default audience for shared notes.png) 4. Auto-share is now turned off for conversations and calendar event meetings. This setting affects all calendar events recorded manually or through Otter Notetaker.  (image: don’t share enabled.png)

Review your individual calendar meetings

Turning off the global settings for your Notetaker settings will affect all calendar event meetings that have not been previously manually set. For individual calendar events where you previously adjusted the auto-share setting, you will need to manually go into each one and turn off auto-share. On the Otter homepage calendar, check your individual calendar events. Click on an event to bring up the settings pop-up and review Share with calendar guests, Share with channels and people, and Share with link settings. (image: review individual calendar settings.jpg) #### Review permission level settings Guests can view or collaborate depending on the default permission you set. Click on the drop-down menu next to Default permission level. Select Collaborator or Viewer. Learn more about managing your share settings. (image: default permission level.png) Set the permission level for allowing Collaborators to share a conversation. Toggle the setting on or off next to Allow Collaborators to share. Learn more about allowing collaborators to share. (image: allow collaborators to share.png) For meetings where you still have auto-join enabled, you can manage the setting to notify meeting participants that your Notetaker is joining. Toggle the setting on or off next to Send pre-recording emails. Learn more about pre-recording emails. (image: send pre-recording emails.png)

Meeting settings & recordings: Troubleshooting

Otter Notetaker is showing as unverified

On February 14th, 2024, Microsoft published a change that will now label guest users as unverified. - No label: All participants who are part of the organizer’s organization. - External: All participants who are external to the organizer’s organization but have a trusted relationship with the organizer or their organization. - Unverified: All other participants will be seen with this label. This will include Microsoft Entra ID users who belong to organizations that do not have explicit external access set up with the organizer’s organization, Microsoft Account (personal) users, users who are not using any Microsoft ID while joining meetings, and others.

Otter Notetaker is only able to join Microsoft Team meetings as a guest participant without a Microsoft ID and cannot sign in to a Microsoft account, which causes Notetaker to fall into the category of unverified. #### Allow guests to join your Microsoft Teams meeting To ensure Notetaker can join your meeting, have your Microsoft Admin enable guest access: 1. Sign in to the Teams admin center. 2. Expand** Meetings** on the navigation menu > Select Meeting Settings. 3. Under Participants, toggle the Anonymous users can join a meeting setting On. 4. Click Save.

Learn more about Microsoft guest settings.

Troubleshooting calendar issues

You can connect your Google or Microsoft account to Otter and sync over your calendar events. If you are experiencing any problems connecting your account or events are not syncing over, this article will provide troubleshooting steps that you can utilize to resolve the issue. #### Unable to connect calendar account If your calendar is not connecting, there may be various settings or issues that could be causing the error. - Try clearing cookies and cache on your browser. Learn how to clear cookies and site data in Chrome, Safari, and Firefox. Once you have cleared cookies and cache, restart your browser, and try connecting your account to Otter. - Ensure your email account is allowed to connect to a 3rd party app. If you are part of a company or organization and your email is provisioned by them, the admin or IT department may be blocking emails from your organization from connecting to 3rd party apps. You will want to check with your department and make sure 3rd party apps are allowed. See the following information on how to check 3rd party access for your account:

Google Calendar 3rd Party Apps - Microsoft Calendar 3rd Party Apps

Calendar events are not syncing over

If your Google or Microsoft account is showing as connected in Apps, but no events are populating in My Agenda, please try the following: - Disconnect your account in Apps > Connect calendars & contact,and try re-connecting your account to refresh the sync. - Open an incognito/private browsing window, log into otter.ai, and check if My Agenda is showing your events properly. If your calendar events are showing properly, clear and cache on your browser. Learn how to clear cookies and site data in Chrome, Safari, and Firefox. Once you have cleared cookies and cache, restarted your browser, check if your events are showing properly. - If specific calendars or calendars events are not showing in My Agenda, ensure they are located under your ‘My Calendars’ in the primary calendar on Google Calendar and Microsoft Calendar. Otter will not be able to sync events from the’Other calendars’ section or from any of the other non-primary calendars.

 (image: calendar_events.png) (image: outlook_events.png)

Otter will not be able to sync events from the following list:

Events located in ‘My Calendars’ if they are not in the primary one (usually one the one at the top or with your name) - Events located in ‘Other calendars’ (calendars you have subscribed to) - Default holiday calendars from Google or Microsoft - Task, Birthdays, or Reminder calendars located under ’My calendars’ from Google

Troubleshooting Notetaker

Otter Notetaker is a powerful AI assistant designed to streamline your tasks and boost productivity. Whether you’re a seasoned Otter user or just getting started, sometimes you might encounter issues that need troubleshooting. This guide is here to assist you in resolving common problems you might face while using Notetaker. #### Server or service disruptions Occasionally, there may be server or service disruptions on the meeting platform’s end, which can impact Notetaker’s ability to join meetings. In such cases, it is advisable to check if there are any reported outages or disruptions with the meeting platform. - Zoom’s status page - Microsoft Team’s status page - Google Meet’s status page

Find your Notetaker’s message error

  1. Navigate to the homepage calendar.
  2. The event may show an error message.
  3. If not, click on the event where Notetaker did not join. Click Open Conversation.

Note: If the event is not there, please scroll up to view the most recent events of that day. 4. Review the Notetaker message.

The host has disabled local recordings for the Zoom meeting. Learn more about the Zoom requirements for Notetaker. (image: host disabled recording for this meeting.png) The host has denied Notetaker from recording the meeting. Learn more about the Zoom requirements for Notetaker. (image: host denied recording access.png) Depending on the meeting settings, participants may not be able to join the meeting until the host joins. In that situation, Notetaker will not be able to join the meeting. Notetaker will wait up to 5 minutes and try up to four times before leaving the meeting. We recommend reaching out to the host and having them enable allow attendees and participants to join the meeting before the host: - Zoom - Allow participants to join before host - Google - Allow participants to join before host - Microsoft Teams - Allow participants to join before host

Please reach out to the host of the meeting and ensure they allow Notetaker to join the meeting. Your host may have set up a waiting room and did not admit Notetaker into the meeting, or removed Notetaker right at the beginning of the meeting. ##### Notify the host Notetaker is joining the meeting You can notify the host 24 hours in advance that Notetaker will be joining the meeting to ensure they will admit Notetaker to the meeting. Manage your settings in Account Settings > Meetings.  (image: email meeting host about OtterPilot joining.png) ##### Other recommendations While meeting platform settings may differ, we recommend the following settings if the option is available: - Allow participants to join before host - Turn off waiting room

The host may have set up a waiting room and did not admit Notetaker into the meeting. Notetaker will continue to attempt to join the meeting for about 12 minutes and then leave the meeting. During the meeting, check to see if any members are waiting to be let into the meeting. A meeting may require a host to approve participants to join. If you are not the host, please make sure you have alerted the meeting host to allow Notetaker to join. - Google Meet waiting room management - Zoom waiting room management - Microsoft Teams waiting room management

Notify the host Notetaker is joining the meeting

You can notify the host 24 hours in advance that Notetaker will be joining the meeting to ensure they will admit Notetaker to the meeting. Manage your settings in Account Settings > Meetings.  (image: email meeting host about OtterPilot joining.png) ##### Other recommendations While meeting platform settings may differ, we recommend the following settings if the option is available: - Allow participants to join before host - Turn off waiting room

Notetaker is not able to join meetings where the host has required the user to sign into the conferencing app. Notetaker will not be able to sign into the meeting platform to join. The meeting must allow external guests for Notetaker to successfully join. - Zoom required authentication

Otter currently supports Zoom, Google Meet, and Microsoft Teams meeting URLs. Check if your meeting is associated with one of the supported platforms by navigating to the meeting event on your home page calendar. Supported meetings will indicate the [platform icon]** + ****[Join meeting]****, as well as the blue Notetaker toggle** ( highlighted green ) . Regular calendar events without a supported link will not show an option for Notetaker to record ( highlighted orange ). ##### Conferencing URL is not valid 1. Copy your meeting URL and navigate to the Otter homepage. 2. In the upper corner, paste the meeting URL into the Paste meeting URL to record field. 3. If your meeting URL is not valid, you’ll see the message Conferencing URL is not valid.

Valid meetings will show Notetaker attempting to join the meeting on the homepage calendar. ##### Meeting URL format Review your meeting URL and ensure there are no typos if you manually create the event. The URL should reflect some variation of the meeting platform, meeting ID, and password. (image: paste_meeting_url.png) To verify your meeting link, copy your meeting URL and open an incognito browser window. Paste and launch the URL to see if it successfully launches to the meeting (e.g. launch window for Zoom, Google Meet, or Microsoft Teams). If the provided meeting credentials, such as the meeting ID or password, are incorrect or invalid, Notetaker won’t be able to join the meeting. It’s important to double-check and ensure that the correct meeting details are entered. ##### Embed passcode Make sure your meeting URL includes the password. Notetaker will not be able to join your meeting without the embedded password if your meeting platform requires it. E.g. The URL should reflect some variation of the meeting platform, meeting ID, and password. (image: paste_meeting_url.png) - Zoom embed passcode

If the meeting URL is scheduled for a time in the future, Notetaker will not be able to join the meeting as it has not started yet. This currently affects Zooms, but may also be applicable to other meeting platforms (e.g. Google Meet and Microsoft Teams). - The meeting takes place on a future date and thus Zoom will not start the meeting. - Zoom generates this error for meetings that were scheduled in the past and will not initiate the meeting.

Generate a new conferencing URL for the present meeting and add it to the calendar event, to sync over the new updated event. Contact the host of the meeting to verify scheduled times and notify them of Notetaker joining the meeting. Some meeting platforms have limitations on the number of participants or concurrent meetings. If the meeting platform has reached its capacity or if there are restrictions on the number of participants, Notetaker may fail to join the meeting. Please reach out to the host and have them confirm the maximum number of participants. ##### My meeting has not reached its capacity, but I’m still seeing this message? The error is likely on the meeting platform’s side. You will want to report and file a support ticket directly with the platform to have them investigate why your meeting is encountering a maximum attendee message. - File with Zoom - File with Google Meet - File with Microsoft Teams

Manually add Notetaker

As a workaround, try manually adding Notetaker using the meeting link. 1. Copy your meeting URL and navigate to the Otter homepage. 2. In the upper corner, paste the meeting URL into the Paste meeting URL to record field.

(image: otter_assistant_manual_add.png) 3. Paste your URL and click Enter/Return or the microphone icon on the right. (image: paste_meeting_url.png)

Please reach out to the host of the meeting and ensure they allow Notetaker to join the meeting. Your host may have set up a waiting room and did not admit Notetaker into the meeting, or removed Notetaker right at the beginning of the meeting. ##### Notify the host Notetaker is joining the meeting You can notify the host 24 hours in advance that Notetaker will be joining the meeting to ensure they will admit Notetaker to the meeting. Manage your settings in Account Settings > Meetings.

(image: email meeting host about OtterPilot joining.png) ##### Other recommendations While meeting platform settings may differ, we recommend the following setting` if the option is available: - Allow participants to join before host - Turn off waiting room

Notetaker will not be able to join your meeting automatically if the event does not sync to Otter. Review yourhome page calendar to make sure all of your events are properly synced over. If you recently added a new event to your calendar and do not see it synced over, click the resync button at the top of the calendar section. If you continue to see that your event is not syncing over, review our calendar troubleshooting guide. Notetaker will leave the meeting if silence is detected during a meeting for up to 12 continuous minutes. If a meeting is running beyond its scheduled time, the silence threshold is reduced to 5 minutes. Check your microphone and see if other participants are able to hear the audio. Check the Otter conversation to see when it stopped transcribing. If you are seeing issues with the conversation, reach out to our Otter Support Team. There may be multiple reasons why Notetaker was not able to join your Microsoft Teams meeting. - Error may be due to Microsoft servers. Check Microsoft’s status page for the most up-to-date info. - URL may not be valid. To verify, copy your meeting URL and open an incognito browser window. Paste and launch the URL to see if it successfully launches Microsoft Teams.

If you are still encountering issues with Notetaker joining your Microsoft Team’s meeting, reach out to our Otter Support Team. The transcription recording limit is 4 hours. Once the limit has been reached, Notetaker will stop recording and transcribing the conversation. If your meeting continues past 4 hours, manually add Notetaker again to the meeting to start a new recording. Check if your meeting has a password. If so, make sure your meeting URL includes the password. Notetaker will not be able to join your meeting without the embedded password if your meeting platform requires it. E.g. The URL should reflect some variation of the meeting platform, meeting ID, and password. (image: paste_meeting_url.png) - Zoom embed passcode

Alternatively, the host can create a meeting room that does not require a password or passcode to enter. There may be conferencing system errors that Notetaker is not able to resolve. If you encounter any of the following error messages, please contact our Otter Support Team. - Otter failed while transcribing meeting - Otter was unable to join your meeting - Zoom restarted while joining your meeting - Otter failed to stream meeting audio, retrying - Otter is unable to customize your Otter participant - Otter is unable to listen to your meeting audio - Conferencing App restarted while joining your meeting - Otter missed the transcription window of your meeting - Otter left your meeting pre-maturely, we are investigating - Otter.ai was unable to join the meeting - Otter is unable to enter your email to join - Otter re-enabled audio - Otter is prevented from joining your meeting at the moment - Otter was unable to launch Zoom - Otter was unable to authenticate Zoom user - Otter is unable to communicate with Zoom - Otter lost connection to the meeting - Your Notetaker waited too long for client to ask it to transcribe meeting - Otter was unable to launch Zoom - Meeting ended for unknown_reasons, transcription was successful - Otter encountered an issue with your meeting invitation - Conferencing system is reporting that this meeting does not exist - Conferencing URL link missing

FAQs

How do I stop the Notetaker from sending a message in the video meeting chat?

  • Notetaker sends a message in the video meeting chat to share the live transcript and summary.
  • You can disable the Notetaker meeting chat feature anytime. To disable this feature: Navigate to Account Settings > Meetings.
  • Under the Notetaker chat messages section, toggle OFF Send live transcript and summary. (image: turn off otterpilot chat messages.png)
  • After making this change, Notetaker will continue to transcribe your meetings but won’t send messages in the video meeting chat.

I need more assistance

If you continue to experience issues with Notetaker after reviewing our troubleshooting guide, reach out to our Otter Support Team.

Otter Workspaces: Getting started

Convert your account to a Workspace domain

Otter Workspaces is the central location for your organization and team. By updating your account to a Workspace domain, you can unlock many more features: - Control how new members enter your Workspace - Surface your Workspace to new and existing Otter members within your email domain - Allow your Workspace members to add new teammates for a free trial period - Auto-share to only users who share your domain

How to convert your account to a Workspace domain

Users that have a generic domain (Gmail.com, Outlook.com, Yahoo.com, iCloud.com, etc.) can convert their Workspace into a Workspace domain (Otter.ai, Amazon.com, Tesla.com, etc.). Only the Owner can convert the Workspace. 1. As the Owner, click on your Workspace name to expand the menu. Select Manage Workspace and navigate to the Settingstab. 2. Click Update email.

Note: If you do not see this option as the Owner, then your Workspace has already been converted to a Workspace domain. 3. Click Edit in the profile section. (image: edit) 4. Click into the Email field and type the email associated with your domain. Click the checkmark when finished.  (image: update) 5. Type in your password and click Validate to complete the email update.  (image: validate.png) 6. Your Workspace should now reflect as a Workspace domain and show the domain features in the Workspace > Manage Workspace > Settingstab. (image: workspace)

Create an Otter Business account

Otter Business brings the power of Otter to small and medium businesses and teams in larger enterprises, including user management, reporting, and more. Learn more about Otter Business. #### Upgrade an existing account 1. Sign in to Otter. 2. Click your name in the top left and click Create Team or** Upgrade plan**. (image: upgrade_plan.png) 3. Use your work email address and the legal name of your organization. (image: enter_business_name.png)

Create a new account

  1. Go to Otter’s pricing page and click Get started under** Business.**
  2. Use your work email address and the legal name of your organization.

💡 Tip: Looking for more information about creating an Otter Business account? This guide will help you to get your team up and running with Otter Business.

Invite people to your Workspace through sharing a conversation

Otter.ai offers many ways to collaborate with team members in your organization, such as sharing a conversation. You now have the option to invite people automatically to your Workspace, when sharing a conversation with them. Users can easily join the Workspaces to start working and collaborating with their team members. #### Getting started To automatically invite people to your Workspace, ensure you have your Workspace set up through your company’s custom domain (e.g. Otter.ai, Amazon.com, Linkedin.com, etc.). You will only be able to automatically invite people who share the same domain as your Workspace. See our How members can join your Workspace article for more information. To begin, as the Admin of the Workspace, review the settings for automatic invites. 1. Click on your Workspace under your name and select Manage Workspace. (image: auto_sharing_1.png) 2. Navigate to the Settings tab next to Analytics. 3. Toggle on Invite teammates to Workspace as free trial members when conversations are shared with them. (image: auto_sharing_2.png)

Now that you have this feature set up, anytime you share a conversation with any user who shares the same domain as you, they will receive an invite to join your Workspace. Once the user accepts the invite, they will join as a trial member. The admin may need to approve the user before they join depending on the Workspace settings. Check out our Understanding billing for trial members in your Workspace (FAQs) article for more information. #### Overview of how it works When sharing a conversation with another user, ensure you type in their work or organization email that matches the Workspace domain you are under. In this example, I am sharing a conversation with otter-demo+vp@otter.ai. That email matches the otter.ai domain of the Workspace, so an invite will be sent out to this user. The user will receive an email to join the Workspace. When they log into their account, they will get a prompt to join the Workspace. The invited user can click Join now to immediately join the Workspace and start using the features of the Workspace, or join later anytime by clicking Join next the Workspace on the left navigation panel. 

(image: auto_sharing_5.png) Depending on if an Admin or Member invited them to the Workspace, they will join either as a trial member or a paid member. Check out our How members can join your Workspace to review your settings.

Looking for more information about creating an Otter workspace? You’ve come to the right place! This guide will help you to get your team up and running. ##### Step 1: Create a workspace First, sign up for an Otter workspace using your work email address. An Otter workspace is a single place for your team and your work. We’ll ask you to verify your email by sending a validation link to your email address. If you have questions about pricing, contact sales. ##### Step 2: Configure workspace and security settings Before inviting team members to join your workspace, we recommend getting familiar with your account’s settings. Check out this video to see a walk-through of your account. ###### Access your team settings To view your workspace settings, follow the steps below in your browser. 1. Sign in to your admin workspace at Otter.ai. 2. Click your Workspace name below your name. 3. Click clickManage Workspace. (image: 3__Manage_workspace.png)

The team settings page enables you to configure your workspace and manage team members. Here are some of the things you can do: - Invite new team members to the team. - Manage a team member including changing their role, deactivating their access, send reminders to pending invites, and managing trials. - View usage analytics (only available for Business & Enterprise) over time, and find out just how much the team is using (and loving) Otter. - Enable additional securitysuch as two-factor authentication.

(image: workspace_members.png) ###### Configure access & security settings For an added layer of security, you can require your members to use two-factor authentication (2FA) when they sign in to Otter. How two-factor authentication works: - Members will get a verification code sent to their mobile device. - To sign in, they’ll enter their verification code along with their password. - Members will need access to enter a verification code sent to their mobile device each time they sign in.

(image: 2fa.png) ##### Step 3: Create a Channel A Channel is a set of members with who you often share a conversation with such as teammates, classmates, etc. When you share a conversation with a Channel, everyone in the Channel will have access to the conversation and be notified via email and push notifications. This saves time by not having to enter the names of each team member individually. We recommend starting with broad Channels for departments (e.g. “Sales Team”) and office locations (e.g. “San Francisco”) initially. ##### Step 4: Invite new team members to your workspace Now that you’ve completed the basics of setting up your workspace, it’s time to bring your workspace to life by inviting team members to join. They will receive an email invitation to join the team. Once they click the link to join, they will sign in with an existing Otter account, or create a new account.

(image: invite_users_to_join_2.png) ##### Step 5: Manage team members and roles Admins can change a role to a team member, deactivate or delete a member’s account, or hide them in the team list view. Admins cannot deactivate or delete another admin, and can only be done by the workspace owner. With more admins, you can share day-to-day tasks with them. Below is an overview of the actions each role can take. - Action: Owner: Admins - Promote admins: (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg): (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg) - Demote admins: (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg): (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg) - Deactivate and reactivate admins: (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg) - Deactivate and reactivate members: (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg): (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg) - Delete members: (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg): (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg) - Hide and unhide members: (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg): (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg) - Resend member invitation: (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg): (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg) - Cancel member invitation: (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg): (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg)

Step 6: Discover the power of apps

Apps connect your Otter workspace with the tools and services you use every day. Click Apps on the left navigation menu to see the available apps. Team members can connect their calendars and contacts to Otter to centralize and streamline their work. You can also connect apps such as Dropbox and Zoom to improve collaboration and save time. (image: apps.png) ###### Pre-approve Otter in the Zoom Marketplace Otter Live Notes integrates with Zoom to make it easy for meeting hosts to instantly start transcribing their meetings, provide a real-time transcription for participants to follow along, and enable participants to take notes collaboratively. Workspace Analytics

Otter workspace analytics gives admins insight into how their team uses Otter. View member activity, transcription minutes, conversations created, and other usage metrics to monitor adoption, analyze trends over time, and better understand workspace engagement. #### Workspace Analytics Overview 1. Click on your profile picture in the upper-left corner, then go to Manage Workspace → Analytics. 

The Analytics page provides an overview of your workspace members and their activity in Otter. Use the selected date range to analyze member adoption, transcription usage, collaboration, and AI Chat activity over time. ##### Member information - Name: The member’s full name. - Email: The member’s email address. - Status: Indicates whether the member currently has an active seat (license or trial) or is deactivated. - Active Within: When the member was last active in the workspace. - Join Date: The date the member joined the workspace.

Usage analytics
  • Minutes Transcribed: Total transcription minutes.
  • Conversations Created: Total conversations created.
  • Average Minutes per Conversation: Average transcription length per conversation.
  • Average Conversations / Month: Average number of conversations created each month.
  • Average Minutes / Month: Average transcription minutes generated each month.
  • AI Chats: Total number of AI Chat messages.
Collaboration Analytics
  • Conversations Viewed: Number of conversations the member viewed.
  • Conversations Shared by User: Number of conversations the member shared with others.
  • Conversations Shared with User: Number of conversations shared with the member.

Click on any of the column headers to sort by that metric. #### Filter by time Filter the time by billing cycle or through a custom date range. - Billing Cycle: Date range based on your billing period. Otter automatically generates these billing periods you can select from. - Between: Select a custom date range for your report.

Deactivated user stats will appear in the report. View usage of past members based on the selected date range. #### Export Analytics Export your current workspace analytics report as a CSV file for further analysis, reporting, or record-keeping. The exported file includes the analytics displayed on the page and can be opened in spreadsheet applications such as Microsoft Excel, Google Sheets, or other compatible data analysis tools. The exported CSV file can be opened in spreadsheet applications such as Microsoft Excel, Google Sheets, or any other application that supports CSV files. #### FAQs #### Why did my numbers change? Workspace Analytics updates based on the selected date range, billing cycle, and the members currently in your workspace. As you change filters or add, remove, activate, or deactivate members, your analytics and summary statistics may change. #### What counts as a conversation? A conversation is counted each time Otter successfully creates a transcript, regardless of how it was recorded or imported. This includes conversations created through the Otter mobile app, desktop app, web app, Otter Notetaker, integrations, and imported audio or video files. #### Why are some analytics not updating for my selected date range? Some analytics are available only for specific reporting periods. For example, Conversations Shared by User and Conversations Shared with User can be viewed only for 30-day, 60-day, or 90-day date ranges. Select one of these supported date ranges to view changes in those metrics. #### Can I export analytics for a specific date range? Yes. Before exporting, select the desired billing cycle or custom date range. The exported CSV includes the analytics displayed for the currently selected reporting period. #### Can I sort the analytics table by any column? Yes. Click most column headers to sort the table in ascending or descending order, making it easier to compare members by different metrics. #### Do AI Chat messages count as conversations? No. AI Chat messages are tracked separately under the AI Chats metric and are not included in the Conversations Created total.

Otter Workspaces: Manage billing, payments & plans

About your Otter Business free trial

When you sign up for a free trial of Otter Business, your team can experience everything that Otter Business offers. - Otter Notetaker - Otter AI Chat - Automated Summaries - Automatic sync of audio/video files from Dropbox

Check out the full list of features for the Otter Business plan on our pricing page. #### Start your free trial To start your Otter Business free trial, navigate to our Get Started for Free page and select the Business Trial. You will be required to enter your payment information, but you will not be charged during the trial period. #### What happens when the trial ends After your trial period is complete, the card on file you entered during sign-up will automatically be charged for the current number of seats in your Workspace, at the Otter Business monthly rate. Alternatively, you may cancel the trial anytime before it expires to avoid any charges. To find out when your trial ends, navigate to Account Settings > Plan.  ##### Cancel your trial If you wish to cancel the trial before it expires, follow the steps below: 1. Click your profile in the upper corner to expand the menu. 2. Select Account Settings and click the Plan tab. 3. Under the Billing Contact section, click Cancel trial. **** 4. In the popup, review what will happen when you cancel your trial. Click Cancel trial to proceed. 5. Your trial is now canceled. Your Workspace will not be charged at the end of the trial period.

Cancel your Otter Business subscription

The owner or admin(s) can cancel an Otter subscription anytime! This will set the subscription to expire at the end of the billing period. Once the paid subscription expires, the workspace can continue to use Otter on the Basic plan. - Subscriptions automatically renew unless you cancel them. - If you cancel, you can keep using the paid features until the subscription expires at the end of the current billing period. - Cancel your subscription at least 24 hours before the next billing date to avoid an unwanted charge. - Applicable limits of the Basic plan apply after the paid subscription expires. - Subscriptions are non-refundable as explained in the Otter Terms of Service.

Steps to cancel your Otter Business subscription

  1. Sign in to Otter.ai as an admin.
  2. Click on Integrations (Account Settings) → Billing, and select Cancel plan at the bottom of the page. 

Tip: Don’t want to fully cancel your subscription, but need a break? Try using the Pause Plan option instead. 3. Select your main reason for cancellation from the options listed, and fill out the box below with more information. 4. Carefully review the information in the pop-up. Click Cancel subscription to confirm.

✅ The subscription has been canceled and will expire at the end of the billing period. You and any member in your workspace can continue to use the paid features until the end of the billing cycle. If you change your mind before the billing cycle ends, reactivate the plan at any time in Account Settings → Billing. Check out our Otter subscription cancellation FAQs for more info. #### 📲 Manage your Business subscription on mobile Canceling your Business subscription is only available through a web browser; however, you can navigate to the mobile web browser within the Otter app. 1. Tap Account. Select Account Settings. 2. Scroll down and tap Manage subscription. **** 3. Click Cancel subscription. **** 4. Click Continue to confirm. **** 5. Once your account has been canceled, you will still have access to your full subscription until the billing period ends.

FAQs

👉 For more information, check out our general subscription cancellation FAQs. #### What happens if I cancel my subscription in the middle of the billing period? Once you cancel your subscription, you can keep using it until the next billing date. Once your billing cycle has ended, the following will happen: - Your workspace will be downgraded to the Basic plan and subject to its limitations. - If a team member is deactivated by an admin, they will not be able to sign in or access their conversations.

Will I lose my conversations if I cancel my subscription?

Your conversations will remain in your account, regardless if you downgrade, upgrade, or cancel your plan. You have full control over your conversations, and they will only be deleted if you choose to delete them, delete your account, or an admin deletes your account if you are part of a Workspace. Access and new conversations are subject to plan limitations (e.g., Basic, Pro, Business).

Purchase additional licenses for your Otter Workspace

When it comes to purchasing additional licenses for your Otter Workspace account, we made it as easy as possible―admins and members can simply invite a team member to the account. Once they sign up and join, we’ll automatically charge you the prorated amount for the time remaining in your billing period. If you have credits in your Otter Workspace account, they will be applied. #### Credit card payments If you are using a credit card as your payment method, we’ll charge you the prorated amount for the time remaining in your billing period. We typically charge and process credit card payments nightly. Example: Your account is on the Otter plan, and you’re paying a per-license amount (monthly or annual). You add a new member 10 days into your billing period, and there are 20 days remaining in the month. We calculate the prorated subscription amount by dividing the cost per license by the number of days in the month (30) and multiplying by the number of days remaining (20). This gives us the prorated amount for the remainder of that billing period. #### Paying by invoice We’ll keep track of how many team members you add and invoice you once per calendar quarter at the prorated amount based on when they were added. The same payment terms as the original invoice will apply. #### Paying by check If your organization can only pay by check, we’ll keep track of how many team members you add and invoice you once per calendar quarter at the prorated amount based on when they were added. The remittance details are included in the invoice. See our vendor and remittance details. #### Requesting a quote or service order If your organization requires a quote or service order form with a purchase order number, please contact sales.

Switching to a different billing cycle for your Business subscription

Switch your Otter subscription to a different billing cycle, such as a monthly plan or annual plan. #### Switch from monthly to annual billing To switch from monthly to annual billing on your current plan, follow these steps: 1. Sign in to your Otter account at otter.ai. 2. Click your name in the top left. 3. Click Account settings and click the Plan tab. 4. Click the Change link, choose the annual plan, and click OK to confirm your change.

(image: updating_monthly_to_annual.png) #### Switch from annual to monthly billing To switch from annual to monthly billing on your current plan, follow these steps: 1. Sign in to your Otter account at otter.ai. 2. Click your name in the top left. 3. Click Account settings and click the Plan tab. 4. Click the Change link, choose the monthly plan, and click OK to confirm your change.

(image: upgrading_to_annual.png)

Understanding billing for trial members in your Workspace (FAQs)

Otter.ai offers an easy way for your team members to integrate our features into their workflows, collaborate with other members in the Workspace, and provide a central location for all of your meeting needs. Both small teams and large organizations can benefit from Otter Workspaces. With our trial process, you and your Workspace members can make an informed decision on the value and benefits Otter.ai can provide for your team. General FAQs to understand your Workspace trials FAQs about billing for subscriptions purchased directly through Otter.ai FAQs about billing for subscriptions purchased through a sales team member #### General FAQs to understand your Workspace trials ##### What happens when I invite members? When a user is invited to join a Workspace through Workspace discovery or a conversation sharing invite, either by an Admin or member, they will join the Workspace as a trial member. Trial members are free to use all of the services and features available with an Otter Workspace. Learn more about how members can join your Workspace. What happens when I remove members? If your total number of licenses decreases, your account will receive a credit on the next billing cycle. The amount of your credit depends on which plan you’re on. Learn more about managing the trials in your Workspace. ##### What if I join as a trial member? If you join as a trial member, you are free to use all of the services reflected in your Workspace subscription free of charge for a limited time. #### FAQs about billing for self-serve subscriptions purchased directly through Otter.ai ##### What happens if someone joins my Workspace as a trial member? When someone joins your Workspace as a trial member, they are free to use all of the services reflected in your subscription for a limited time. The date of the end of their trial will appear in your Workspace Settings, which is when they will convert to paid members. If you don’t want to pay for them to become licensed members, an Admin can cancel their membership. ##### How is pricing calculated for the Otter plan? Pricing is calculated per license, or member for either the monthly or annual duration. Please review the pricing for each license on our pricing page. ##### How are my payments processed? What is Stripe?  Stripe is one of the largest and most secure online payment processors available. Otter.ai uses Stripe to process your online payments. We never collect, view, or store any of your credit card information. ##### Can I get an invoice? To see if you qualify for invoicing, please contact our sales team. ##### What if my payment is declined? If your payment is declined, you’ll receive an email notification and we’ll retry the charge. We’ll attempt to charge your card up to three (3) times. After the third try, if payment is still unsuccessful, your account will be downgraded to the Otter Basic (free) plan. #### FAQs about billing for Business and Enterprise subscriptions purchased through Otter Sales ##### What happens when I invite members, or if someone joins my Workspace as a trial member? When a user joins through Workspace Discovery or is invited to join the Workspace through a shared conversation invite, either by an Admin or member, they will join the Workspace as a trial member. Trial members are free to use all of the services and paid features of the Business or Enterprise subscription. Learn more about how members can join your Workspace. ##### How is pricing calculated for my subscription? Pricing is calculated per license, or member. Please speak with Otter Sales to obtain the details of your subscription pricing. ##### When will I be charged for my trial members? Please speak with Otter Sales to move forward with converting your trials to paid licenses. You have full control over which trial members convert to paid. Before converting to paid licenses, remove any trial members that you do not want to purchase a license for. Learn more about managing the trials in your Workspace. #### Learn more about your Workspace features and management - How members can join your Workspace - Managing your Workspace trial members - Managing your team members in a Workspace - Invite new members to join your account

Understanding how seats (licenses) work

A seat is a license that you purchase to assign to your Otter Business users. One seat is used by one user. When you sign up for Otter Business, you specify the number of seats to purchase, and those seats are added to your Otter Business account. When a user is invited and joins the account, they are counted against the available seats. #### Purchasing additional seats If all available seats are in use, we made it as easy as possible to add more users―admins and members can simply invite a team member to the account. Once they sign up and join, we’ll automatically charge you the prorated amount for the time remaining in your billing period. If you have credits in your Otter Business account, they will be applied. Learn more about purchasing additional seats. #### Deactivating or deleting a user If a user is deactivated or deleted from the account, they will no longer count towards a seat and will free up a seat. However, if the Otter Business account exceeded the initial number of seats and a user is deactivated or deleted, we’ll add prorated credits to their account. The credit will be applied when purchasing additional seats or on their renewal date. These credits have no currency or exchange value, are non-transferable and non-refundable, and will expire following the termination of your paid services plan with Otter. Example (the number of seats never exceeded the initial seats purchased): Acme, Inc. purchased the Otter Business plan for 10 seats. They invite 10 team members and therefore, all seats are counted for and in use. An admin deactivates 1 user. A seat is now available within the account. No credits will be added to the account. Example (the number of seats exceeded the initial seats purchased): Hooli Corp purchased the Otter Business plan for 20 seats. They invite 20 team members and therefore, all seats are in use. An admin invites 1 additional team member and is charged a prorated amount. Their seat minimum is adjusted to 21 seats. An admin deactivates 1 user. A prorated credit is added to the account and the seat minimum is adjusted to 20 seats. #### How a prorated credit is calculated We calculate the prorated credit by dividing the cost per seat by the total number of days in the billing period and multiplying by the number of days remaining in your billing period. Example: Your account is on the Otter Business plan and you’re paying yearly. You deactivate a user 180 days into your billing period and there are 185 days remaining in the billing period. We calculate the prorated credit amount by dividing the cost per seat by the number of days in the year (365) and multiplying by the number of days remaining (185). This gives us the prorated credit amount for the remainder of the billing period. ($ amount / 365) x 185 = prorated credit #### Renewing your Otter Business subscription Here’s what happens at the end of your subscription: - If you don’t do anything, your subscription automatically renews on the same term (one month or year) for the initial seat commitment. - If you chose the option not to renew by canceling your subscription, your service will be suspended. You can lift the suspension and renew your subscription by reactivating your plan on the Account Settings page. If you need assistance, contact support.

We recommend you make sure your billing account is up to date so your service won’t be interrupted when it’s time to renew. If you don’t do anything, your initial seat commitment will automatically continue on your next renewal date.

Otter Workspaces: Manage members

Change a member’s role & permission

As an owner or administrator for your Otter Business account, you can promote other members to help manage your account. - Owners can deactivate or delete another admin and demote an admin. There can only be one owner of an account, but this can be transferred to another member. All owners are admins by default. - Admins can change a role to a team member’s account, hide and unhide members, and promote members as admin, as well as demote themselves back to a member role.

Choose your account owner and admins

Promoting trusted members to owner and admin roles can help with managing your Otter account. With more admins, you can share day-to-day tasks such as inviting new team members. For the account owner, we recommend it to be: - Founder or executive within a small startup - Senior leadership of a specific department - IT department for the entire organization

For admins, we recommend: - Managers - Senior administrative staff - Project managers - IT engineers or specialists

Who can manage roles

Only admins can manage roles for other members. Below is an overview of the actions each role can take. - Action: Owner: Admins - Promote to owner: (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg) - Promote admins: (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg): (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg) - Demote admins: (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg): (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg) - Deactivate and reactivate admins: (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg) - Deactivate and reactivate members: (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg): (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg) - Delete members: (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg): (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg) - Hide and unhide members: (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg): (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg) - Resend member invitation: (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg): (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg) - Cancel member invitation: (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg): (image: check_circle-black-24dp.svg)

The previous owner’s role will be ‘Admin’. (image: Screen_Shot_2022-10-19_at_9.56.37_PM.png)

The information above will verify ownership of the Workspace and our team will be able to process your request. Deactivate a member in a workspace

Deactivate a member to remove access while preserving their data and keeping them in your workspace. This approach is ideal for offboarding, temporary leave, role changes, or compliance reviews where conversations, transcripts, and shared content must remain available. Deactivation offers more control than removing or deleting a member, which can permanently remove data needed for collaboration, auditing, or reference. #### What happens to a deactivated member’s data? When an admin deactivates a member, the following occurs: - No data is deleted when the user is deactivated. All existing data is preserved. - The user remains in the Workspace. - The member no longer counts as a licensed user. - Sign-in access is disabled immediately. - Previously shared conversations remain accessible (e.g., in Channels or via direct shares).

Deactivate a member

Follow the steps below to deactivate a member anytime. 1. Sign in to Otter.ai as an admin. Click on your profile and select Manage Workspace. 2. Click the three-dot menu next to the user you want to deactivate, then select Revoke access to Workspace. 3. Carefully review the pop-up. Click Revoke access to proceed. Learn more about what happens to a user’s data when they’re deactivated. 4. User is immediately deactivated.

Accessing shared conversations from deactivated users Previously shared remain accessible

All conversations the user previously shared remain accessible after deactivation, including conversations shared to Channels or via direct share. All existing sharing permissions remain unchanged after deactivation (for example, if a conversation was shared with Viewer access, it will remain accessible as Viewer). The following conversation sharing settings are preserved: - Collaborator and Viewer access - Export settings - Public link settings - Collaborator settings

Reactivating a member Reactivate a user anytime

Admins can reactivate a user anytime in Workspace > Members. Click on the three-dot menu next to the deactivated user and select Reactivate. - Existing conversations, transcripts, and shared content are restored as they were prior to deactivation. - The member will be immediately counted as a licensed user.

FAQs

What happens to a member’s data when they’re deactivated?

What changes when a member is deactivated: - The member can no longer sign in to Otter. - The member no longer counts as a licensed user. - Access is revoked immediately after deactivation.

What stays the same: - No conversations, transcripts, or shared content are deleted. - The member remains deactivated and listed in the Workspace. - Previously shared conversations (such as Channels or direct shares) remain accessible to collaborators. - Ownership and history of conversations are preserved.

When should an admin deactivate a member instead of removing them?

  • Admins should deactivate a member when access needs to be restricted without deleting data, such as during employee offboarding, temporary leave, role changes, or compliance-related reviews. Removing a member is more appropriate when their data no longer needs to be retained.

Can I still access a deactivated member’s shared conversations?

  • Yes.
  • All conversations the user previously shared remain accessible after deactivation.

Does deactivation affect SSO or identity provider access?

  • Yes. Once deactivated, the user cannot sign in via SSO or any other sign-in method, even if their identity still exists in the identity provider (IdP).
  • In addition to deactivating the user in Otter, admins should complete any required offboarding steps in their identity provider (IdP).

Can conversation ownership or permissions be changed after a user is deactivated?

  • If you need to change conversation ownership or adjust permissions (such as Collaborator or Viewer access, export settings, or public link settings), have a Workspace admin contact our Otter Support Team.

Does deactivation immediately revoke access?

  • Yes. Deactivation immediately disables the user’s ability to sign in to Otter.
  • The user will no longer be able to access their account or any conversations.
  • The member will encounter the ” User has been deactivated error when signing in.

Can collaborators still access conversations shared by a deactivated user?

  • Yes. Any conversations that were shared before deactivation remain accessible to other users, such as through Channels, direct shares, or public links, based on the existing permission level.

What happens to a user’s data if they are reactivated?

When a user is reactivated, their existing conversations, transcripts, and shared content are restored as they were prior to deactivation. #### Do existing sharing permissions change after deactivation? - No. All existing sharing permissions remain unchanged after deactivation. For example, if a conversation was shared with Viewer or Collaborator access, that permission remains in place.

Does a deactivated user still consume a license?

  • No. A deactivated user no longer counts as a licensed user or seat.
  • Depending on your contract or plan, their license may become available for reassignment.

Delete a member from your Workspace

An admin can permanently delete a user from the Workspace. - We recommend deactivating or removing the user from the Workspace, if there is any info that needs to be kept in the account. Learn more about managing members in your Workspace. - A prorated credit will be issued to the Workspace billing account, based on the remainder of the subscription period, for the team member that was deleted. This credit can be applied to additional seat licenses, renewals, or purchases. Learn more about how seats work.

Follow these steps to delete a member from the Workspace: 1. Ensure you are signed into Otter as an admin. 2. Navigate to Workspace> Manage Workspace > Members. 3. Click the 3 dots for the team member that needs to be deleted. 4. Select Delete forever. (image: delete_a_member.png) 5. In the pop-up, select Delete to confirm. (image: confirm_deletion.png) 6. The user’s account is now permanently deleted. All conversations, settings, and info have been permanently deleted.

Manage your workspace members

An admin has multiple options for managing a member in a workspace. A member can be deactivated, removed, or deleted from a workspace. #### Revoke access to the workspace Admins can manage a workspace member’s access in several ways, including removing a member from the workspace, temporarily suspending access while retaining their data, and, if needed, permanently deleting the member’s account and data after deactivation. 1. Go to Profile → Manage Workspace. 2. Click the three-dot menu next to the member whose access you want to revoke, then select Revoke access to workspace.

Note: Workspace admins cannot be deactivated, removed, or deleted directly. To revoke an admin’s access, first change their role to ‘Member’. 3. You can remove the user or deactivate the account. Follow the steps below for more information.

Deactivate a user (recommended) Deactivate a member and keep them in your workspace

We recommend deactivating users to remove their access while retaining their accounts and data within the workspace. Deactivated users can no longer sign in to or access their Otter account, but all recordings, conversations, and workspace data are preserved. No data is deleted, and an admin can reactivate the user at any time if access needs to be restored. 1. Follow the steps in Revoke access to a workspace. 2. In the pop-up, select Deactivate account and keep in Workspace, then click Revoke access. 3. The member has been deactivated.

Reactivate a member  Reactivate a member in your workspace

You can reactivate a deactivated member at any time. Once reactivated, the user regains access to their account, and all conversations, data, and account settings are restored exactly as they were before deactivation. 1. Click the three-dot menu next to the member you want to reactivate, then select Reactivate member. 2. Click Reactivate to confirm. 3. The member has been reactivated.

Remove a member from your workspace Removed members can continue using Otter as an individual account

Remove the member from the workspace and convert their account to an individual Otter account. The user retains access to the recordings, conversations, and other content they created while in the workspace and can continue using Otter or purchase their own subscription. Conversations that were previously shared by the user will remain accessible to existing collaborators. 1. Follow the steps in Revoke access to a workspace. 2. In the pop-up, select Remove account from Workspace, then click Revoke access. 3. The member has been removed from the workspace.

When a member is removed

After being removed from a workspace, the member’s account is converted to an individual Otter account. They can continue using Otter independently and may upgrade to a paid subscription if they need additional features or higher usage limits. If the member was removed unintentionally, a workspace admin can re-invite them to the workspace. Refer to our pricing page for the latest plan features and limits. #### Delete a member from your workspace Permanently delete a user and all data If you need to preserve the member’s data or may need to restore access in the future, we recommend deactivating the user instead. Deactivated users cannot access their account, but their data remains available within the workspace. Only delete a member if you are certain their account and data are no longer needed. 1. Follow the steps in Deactivate a user. 2. Click the three-dot menu next to the deactivated member, then select Delete member. 3. Carefully review the pop-up and click Delete to confirm. 4. The member has been permanently deleted.

FAQs

Can I recover a deleted account?

No. Deleting an account is permanent and cannot be undone. All recordings, conversations, account settings, and other account data are permanently deleted and cannot be recovered. If you need to retain a user’s data, we recommend deactivating the user instead of deleting their account. #### What’s the difference between removing, deactivating, and deleting a member? - Remove a member: Removes the user from the workspace and converts their account to an individual Otter account. The user retains access to their content and can continue using Otter independently. - Deactivate a member: Prevents the user from accessing their account while preserving all data within the workspace. An admin can reactivate the user at any time. - Delete a member: Permanently deletes the user’s account and all associated data. This action cannot be reversed.

Why can’t I deactivate, remove, or delete an admin?

To help prevent accidental loss of administrative access, workspace admins cannot be deactivated, removed, or deleted directly. If you need to revoke an Admin’s access, first change their role to Member. Once their role has been updated, you can deactivate, remove, or delete the account as needed. Learn more about managing roles.
