  1. Tap on Account. Select Account Settings.
  2. Tap Conversation Language.
  3. Select the default language. Tap the back icon ** **to save your selection.

Note: German and Chinese (Simplified) are currently still in beta.

✅ Conversations will be transcribed in that language going forward. #### Japanese language #### FAQs #### I changed the language. Will it update the user interface (UI) in Otter as well? No. Changing the language will only affect transcription processing for a conversation. Other parts of Otter UI, such as settings, navigation, and menus, will all remain in English. We hope to bring full UI language support in future updates. #### Can I record in multiple languages for a conversation? No. At this time, you will need to select the default language before recording. If you have selected English, but need to record in Spanish for the next meeting, you will need to update the setting first to record in Spanish. Our team is working on supporting multi-language recording in future updates. Stay tuned! #### Do you support transcribing in French and English simultaneously? Yes. You can transcribe both French and English simultaneously. Otter supports auto-switching to English if the default language is set to French. If your meetings are primarily in French and English, we recommend setting the default language to French. #### Do you support transcribing in other languages with English simultaneously besides French? #### I forgot to change the language before a meeting. Can I reprocess it in a different language? No. At this time, there is no way to reprocess a conversation into a different language post-recording. The conversation will be processed in the language set at the beginning of the recording. Re-import the conversation audio file to transcribe in another language. 1. Export the conversation audio file in Otter. 2. Follow the steps above to switch your language to the correct one. 3. Import the audio file into Otter to be transcribed in the set language.

I changed the language halfway through a meeting. Will that meeting be recorded in the new language?

No. The conversation will be transcribed in the language that was set at the start of the recording. If you switch the language partway through the recording, it will continue to transcribe in the language that was set at the start of the recording. #### Do you support any other languages? We currently only support transcribing in English, Spanish, French, German, Japanese, and Chinese (Simplified); however, we hope to support more languages in the future. Stay tuned! #### What is the difference between English (US) and British English (UK)? Selecting an English dialect will reflect the most common spelling for that dialect when transcribing a conversation. #### Which Chinese languages are supported for transcription? We currently support spoken Mandarin Chinese. We hope to support more languages and dialects in the future such as Cantonese and other dialects. Stay tuned! #### Can I transcribe in Chinese (Traditional)? We currently support transcription for spoken Mandarin with Simplified Chinese output.

Transcription processing time FAQ

After you finish recording a conversation or successfully upload an audio file, the audio processing begins automatically. Once processing is complete, you can view transcripts, edit conversations, export meeting notes, collaborate with team members, and view AI-powered summaries and insights. The audio processing system uses advanced machine learning to identify speakers, separate conversations into speaker-specific segments, and organize dialogue using voice characteristics and timestamps. This speaker recognition and transcription process improves the accuracy of meeting transcripts, conversation summaries, and searchable notes, though processing times may vary depending on audio length and the number of speakers. Learn more about speaker learning. #### How long does processing take? Processing time varies depending on the length of the recording or uploaded file, the number of speakers detected, and current system demand on the Otter service. The table below outlines the estimated processing times for each supported input type, based on a 30-minute meeting. - Input Type (based on a 30-minute recording): Estimated Transcript Processing Time: Estimated AI Summary & Email Processing Time - Otter Notetaker, Live Notes, ad-hoc manual recording: 1.5 – 3 minutes: 2.5 – 6 minutes - Imported audio and video files: 4.5 – 6 minutes: 5.5 – 7 minutes - Offline ad-hoc recording (mobile): 4.5 – 6 minutes: 5.5 – 7 minutes

  • Processing begins after the file is successfully uploaded. ** Processing begins after reconnecting to the internet, and the file is successfully uploaded. #### Imported file(s) processing time For imported files, transcript and AI summary processing only begin after the file has been fully uploaded to Otter. Once the upload is complete, Otter will display the processing progress percentage. Refer to our chart for estimated processing times based on a typical 30-minute meeting recording. #### FAQs #### How long does Otter take to process a meeting? For a 30-minute meeting recorded by Otter, a draft transcript is available in real time during the meeting. After the meeting ends, the finalized transcript is typically available within 1.5 to 3 minutes, and the summary within 2.5 to 6 minutes. #### How long does Otter take to process an imported file? For a 30-minute recording imported into Otter, the transcript is typically generated within 4.5 – 6 minutes, and the summary within 5.5 – 7 minutes.** Processing begins after the file is successfully uploaded. #### How long does Otter take to process an offline recording? For a 30-minute offline recording by Otter, the transcript is typically generated within 4.5 – 6 minutes** of import, and the summary within 5.5 – 7 minutes.** **Processing begins after reconnecting to the internet, and the file is successfully uploaded. #### Why is my conversation still processing? Processing time depends on the length of the recording or file, the number of speakers, and current demand on the Otter service. During peak usage, even shorter recordings may take longer than usual to process. View our Status Page for any updates on outages. If your conversation has been processing for several hours, it may be experiencing an unexpected delay. If this happens, please contact our Otter support team for assistance. Be sure to include the conversation title, date, and time so we can help resolve the issue more quickly. #### Why won’t my file import or process? When you import an audio or video file into Otter.ai, a progress indicator displays the upload status and percentage completed. Transcript generation and AI summary processing only begin after the file has been fully uploaded to Otter’s servers. If the upload progress remains stuck for an extended period or an error appears, it may be caused by:
  • An unstable or slow internet connection
  • Network interruptions or VPN/firewall restrictions
  • Unsupported or corrupted audio/video files
  • Large file sizes or limited device resources

Ensure your internet connection remains stable and keep the Otter.ai app or browser window open while the file is uploading. After the upload is successfully completed, you can safely close the app or browser, as transcript and AI summary processing continue on our Otter servers. If the progress indicator is showing the same percentage for a longer than expected time or shows an error, there may be a problem with your internet connection or other factors.

Troubleshoot audio recording problems

If Otter isn’t recording audio, missing certain speakers, or producing incomplete transcripts, the issue is usually related to microphone permissions, browser or device settings, headphones, network connectivity, or audio suppression software. This guide walks through the most common causes and provides step-by-step troubleshooting for Otter Web, Windows, macOS, iPhone, and Android. #### Otter isn’t recording any audio If Otter isn’t detecting audio from your microphone, it won’t be able to record or transcribe your conversation. Follow the troubleshooting steps below to identify and resolve the issue. #### Troubleshooting on desktop What device are you experiencing this issue on? #### Troubleshoot on Windows or Mac Review the troubleshooting steps for Windows and Mac devices If Otter isn’t recording audio on Windows, first verify that your microphone is configured correctly and that microphone permissions are enabled. Windows settings UI may change over time depending on your OS and version. Refer to Microsoft’s official documentation for the most up-to-date instructions. 1. Open the Start menu and go to Settings → System → Sound →** Volume mixer. Under Input device, make sure the correct microphone is selected and that it is receiving audio. 2. Go to Settings** → Privacy & security → Microphone. Make sure the Otter desktop app and any browser you are using with Otter have access to your microphone. 3. Test a new recording in the Otter desktop app or web browser to confirm that audio is being detected. If audio still isn’t recording on your browser, review your browser microphone settings for additional troubleshooting.

Audio is still not working in Windows

If your microphone is configured correctly but Otter still isn’t recording audio, the issue may be caused by your Windows audio settings or drivers. Follow Microsoft’s official troubleshooting guide, starting with the first recommendation and continuing until the issue is resolved. Follow these steps to review your microphone input settings. For the most up-to-date steps regarding sound management for your macOS system, refer to Apple’s documentation. 1. Open Systems Settings.  2. Search or navigate to Sound.   3. Under the Output & Input section, make sure the microphone device you want to use is selected.

Note: While Otter needs your microphone to record audio, make sure your speaker output level is set as well to ensure your microphone will pick up audio from your speakers. 4. ✅ Make sure your input volume is not set at zero and your input level has activity. 5. Next, navigate to Privacy & Security. Select Microphone. 6. Make sure the microphone permission is toggled ON for the browser you’ll be recording Otter with.

Check your browser’s microphone permissions Verify that your browser (Chrome, Safari, Firefox, etc.) has the correct permissions

  • Chrome: Microphone settings
  • Firefox: Microphone settings
  • Safari: Microphone settings

Review your browser’s microphone settings. We’ll be using Chrome as an example. 1. Open your browser’s Settings. 2. Go to Privacy & security → Site settings. Under Permissions, select Microphone. 3. Verify that: The correct microphone is selected as the input device. 4. https://otter.ai is allowed to access your microphone for website recordings. 5. Otter.ai: Record & Transcribe Meetings (Chrome Extension) is allowed to access your microphone if you’re using the extension. 6. Return to Otter and test a new recording to verify that audio is being detected.

Audio is still not recording Check for audio suppression settings and applications that may be blocking recording

If the above troubleshooting steps did not resolve the issue, follow these steps below to narrow down if the issue is affecting your device, browser, or just Otter.ai. ##### Audio Suppression Settings If Otter isn’t recording audio from your speakers, other meeting participants, or video playback, the issue may be caused by noise suppression or echo cancellation features on your computer, mobile device, headset, or conferencing application. These features are designed to remove background noise and prevent echo during calls. However, they can mistakenly identify audio playing through your speakers as background noise and prevent Otter from recording or transcribing it. To test whether audio suppression is causing the issue, temporarily disable any noise suppression or echo cancellation features, then start a new recording in Otter. Depending on your device or application, these settings may be labeled as: - Noise suppression - Noise cancellation - Echo cancellation - Reduced background noise

The following devices and applications are known to include audio suppression features: - Dell computers - HP computers - Krisp.ai - Microsoft Teams application

Isolate the root issue
  1. Test your device. Try using a local voice recorder application that comes with your operating system. If your local recording is not able to pick up audio, then we recommend reaching out to your device manufacturer’s support for more troubleshooting steps.  Record in Windows Voice Recorder
  2. Record in MacOS Voice Memos
  3. Test your browser. Try recording using a different service in your browser to test if the audio issue is affecting just Otter or all web applications. If the issue is affecting other applications in your web browser, we recommend doing a complete re-install of your browser or try using a different browser.Record in Online Voice Recorder

Otter is only recording my voice Headphones and browser settings can cause only your voice to be recorded

If Otter is only transcribing your voice and not other speakers, meeting participants, or audio playing from your computer, the issue is often caused by headphones, browser recording limitations, or audio suppression features. ##### Headphones and browser recording When recording manually in a web browser, Otter relies on your device’s microphone to capture audio. If you’re wearing headphones, earbuds, or a headset, meeting or playback audio is sent directly to your headphones instead of your device’s speakers. Because your microphone can’t hear that audio, Otter may record only your voice. Example: You’re transcribing a Zoom, Google Meet, or Microsoft Teams on the web browser meeting while wearing headphones. - The meeting audio is played only through your headphones. - Your device’s microphone can’t capture the other participants’ voices because the audio isn’t coming from your speakers. - Otter records your voice, but may not transcribe the other meeting participants.

Recommended solutions

Try one of the following: - Use the Otter desktop app. It can capture system audio, allowing Otter to transcribe audio playing on your computer without relying on your microphone. - Use Otter Notetaker for Zoom, Google Meet, and Microsoft Teams meetings. Notetaker captures the meeting audio directly and doesn’t rely on your device’s microphone or speakers. - Import the audio or video file directly into Otter instead of recording playback whenever possible. - If you’re recording audio that can’t be imported, remove your headphones so your device’s microphone can capture the audio playing through your speakers.

Safari recording issues

Apple prevents recording any audio playback (e.g., audio coming out of the device speaker) in the Safari browser. Meeting participant voices coming out of the speaker will not be picked up by the microphone, as Apple prevents it from being recorded. This is a limitation set by Apple. Our recommendation is to record in Chrome or Firefox if you are recording manually in Otter. #### Otter isn’t recording computer audio (e.g., YouTube, video playback) Troubleshoot transcribing audio played through your computer When recording manually in a web browser, Otter relies on your device’s microphone to capture audio. If you’re wearing headphones, earbuds, or a headset, audio from your meeting, video, or speakers is played directly to your headphones instead of through your device’s speakers. As a result, your microphone may only capture your voice, causing Otter to miss other speakers or playback audio. Example: You’re transcribing a YouTube video while wearing headphones. - The YouTube audio is played only through your headphones. - Your device’s microphone can’t hear the audio because it isn’t coming from the speakers. - Otter records your voice but may not capture the video’s audio.

Recommended solutions
  • Use the Otter desktop app. The desktop app can capture system audio, allowing Otter to transcribe audio playing on your computer while you wear headphones.
  • If you’re transcribing an audio or video file, import the file directly into Otter for the best transcription quality.

📱 Mobile app audio troubleshooting

Review the following troubleshooting steps for the Otter mobile apps on iOS and Android. Many of the issues can be resolved through permission settings. We always recommend checking your settings to verify that Otter has the correct permissions. ##### Ensure Otter has microphone permissions 1. If Otter does not have microphone permissions, you will not be able to start a recording. A pop-up will appear to alert you. Tap Go to Settings to open your device’s microphone settings. 2. Make sure microphone permission has been granted for the Otter mobile app. 3. Start a recording to test if audio is being detected successfully.

Otter is not picking up my Bluetooth device’s audio (AirPods, headphones, headsets, external microphone, etc.)

Review the Bluetooth settings for Otter. 1. Ensure your Bluetooth device is connected to your mobile device. Refer to your device’s documentation on how to connect your Bluetooth device. 2. In the Otter mobile app, tap on Account. Select Account Settings.  3. Tap on Advanced settings.  4. Toggle ON Record via Bluetooth. 5. Start a recording to test if audio is being detected successfully.

Slow network, no transcript

Otter requires a stable internet connection to provide live transcription. If your device has experienced a network disruption or loss, Otter will continue to record, but will not be able to provide a live transcription. Once connected again, Otter will attempt to upload the audio and provide a transcription. ##### Send diagnostics If you’re having any recording issues with a conversation, including audio issues, missing parts of a conversation, or missing duration length, please send diagnostics and contact our Otter Support Team. 1. Tap on Account. Select Account Settings.  2. Scroll down and tap Send diagnostics. 3. Select the diagnostic recording(s) you’re having trouble with. Click Send in the upper right corner. The diagnostic(s) will begin uploading. Once the diagnostic(s) have been sent, you’ll see a pop-up confirmation. 4. Submit a request and contact our Otter Support Team. Include in the request that you have already sent diagnostics.

✨ Tips for improving transcription accuracy

Follow these best practices to improve transcription accuracy and help Otter capture every speaker more reliably. - Reduce background noise. Record in a quiet environment whenever possible. - Use an external microphone. A dedicated USB microphone typically provides better audio quality than a built-in microphone. Note: Some external microphones, headsets, and Bluetooth devices may have limitations depending on your device, browser, or operating system. - Keep the microphone close. Position it within 3 feet (1 meter) of the speaker and avoid moving or touching it during recording. - Avoid people talking over each other. Overlapping speech makes it more difficult for Otter to distinguish and transcribe individual speakers. - Speak naturally. Use your normal conversational pace and volume. - Add specialized vocabulary. Teach Otter company names, product names, technical terms, and other jargon to improve recognition. - Train Otter to recognize your voice. Voice training helps Otter identify you more accurately and automatically label your speaker name in transcripts.

FAQs

Why isn’t Otter recording any audio?

If Otter isn’t recording audio, first verify that your microphone is connected, selected as the input device, and that Otter has permission to access it. Then review your operating system, browser, and microphone settings to identify any configuration issues. Review our troubleshooting audio steps. #### Why is Otter only recording my voice? If Otter records only your voice, your microphone may not be able to capture audio from other participants or your computer’s speakers. This commonly occurs when wearing headphones, using certain browser configurations, or when noise suppression or echo cancellation is enabled. We recommend using Notetaker** or desktop app to wear headphones while recording. #### Why isn’t Otter recording other meeting participants? If other participants aren’t being transcribed, check whether: - You’re wearing headphones. - Noise suppression or echo cancellation is enabled. - Your microphone is too far away from the speakers. - You’re manually recording instead of using Notetaker **or desktop app

Why isn’t Otter recording audio from YouTube or other videos?

When recording manually in a web browser, Otter relies on your microphone to capture audio. If the audio is playing through headphones or your browser blocks system audio capture, Otter may not hear it. We recommend using the d esktop app or importing the audio or video file directly into Otter whenever possible.

Using Otter: Review & edit conversations

Add comments to your conversation

Add, reply, and interact with comments to start conversations with your team members. #### Add a comment 1. In the Transcript tab of a conversation, highlight any content you want to leave a comment on to bring up the toolbar. 2. Click Comment (******) in the toolbar. 3. Type your comment and click the send button () on the right or hit Enter/Return on your keyboard. To notify another Otter user of your comment, tag them using @. 4. On the panel right of the conversation page, click the Comments** tab to view all comment threads you or your team members created.

Note: Comments cannot be sorted or reordered at this time.

Reply to comments

In the Comments tab, click into the Reply field to reply to any comment threads. Type your response and click the send button () on the right or hit Enter/Return on your keyboard to reply. To notify another Otter user of your comment, tag them using** @**. You can react to comments using emojis. #### Copy link or delete a comment You can generate a link to the comment  or delete a comment anytime by clicking the 3-dots () next to a comment: - Copy Link: Generates a direct link to the comment.

Note: Any users you share the link to will need collaborator permissions to view the comment. - Delete: Permanently deletes a comment. Deleted comments are not recoverable.

Add highlights to your conversation

Highlight content during the live recording or post-recording to call out parts of the conversation you want to revisit or to remember important information. Select the content you want to highlight. Click highlight (). To quickly highlight, click highlight () on the controls panel and Otter will automatically highlight the current portion of the transcript either during playback or the most recently transcribed section during the live recording. #### View only your highlights View all of your highlights in one central location. On the Transcript tab, toggle on Show only highlights to view all highlights. You can playback and review just the highlights of your transcript.

Add images to your conversation

Add images to your conversations during the live recording or post-recording. 1. Highlight the text you want to add an image to and click Add image (******)** in the toolbar or control panel. 2. You have the option to upload from your device or paste from your clipboard. 3. Select the file you want to add. Click Open. (image: addimage.gif) 4. The image will be inserted next to the text.

Audio playback is not working

Read through these troubleshooting steps when you encounter issues with audio playback. #### Clear Cache Try clearing cookies and cache on your browser for web users. Learn how to clear cookies and site data in Chrome, Safari, and Firefox. Once you have cleared cookies and cache, restart your browser, and try clicking the play button in your conversation. (image: playback.png) #### Check your internet connection Audio playback may not work properly if your network is unstable. - Check your upload speed and ensure it matches what you subscribed to with your internet service provider (ISP). We recommend upload speeds of at least 3 Mbps. (image: IMG_4233.PNG) - Try connecting to a different network such as your mobile phone hotspot or disconnect your Wi-Fi (on mobile) to see if audio playback works.

Need additional help?

If these didn’t fix your issue, the Otter Support Team for further assistance. Provide as much detail as you can, along with any screenshots or screen recordings. If playback is working on one device, but not the other, note that in the support request form.

Automated Live Summary Overview

Generate a summary in real-time during a live event, meeting, or conversation allowing participants to quickly grasp key points and important information as they unfold. Improve overall comprehension and improve engagement without being overwhelmed by an excessive amount of information. Otter’s proprietary AI will automatically generate a live summary during a recording and display it in the Automated Summary tab of the conversation so you and your colleagues can quickly scan over the main topics #### View the Automated Summary for a conversation 1. Navigate to the conversation during or after the recording that you want to view the summary for. 2. Click the Summary tab on the Takeaways panel to open the Automated Summary tab. (image: summary tab.png) 3. During a live recording, the summary will automatically generate in real-time. (image: live summary.gif)

Note: The automated summary evaluates the conversation every ~3 minutes, but depending on how much content there is there might not be enough to generate a summary, in which case it will skip and attempt to generate at a later time. 4. Click on a topic to navigate to that specific timestamp in the conversation. (image: navigating summary.gif)

Edit chapter summary titles

Click on any of the titles to edit and click Enter/Return when finished. Delete a conversation

Otter lets you easily delete your conversations at any time. Learn what to expect and how to delete a conversation in Otter. When a conversation is deleted: - Conversation(s) will be immediately unshared from all users and Channels. - Conversation(s) will be moved to the Trash folder for 30 days. - After 30 days, the conversation and all associated data will be permanently deleted from our servers.

Delete a conversation

Follow the instructions below to delete a conversation on the desktop or web version of Otter. 1. Go to the conversation you want to delete, click the three-dot menu, and select Delete. 2. Carefully review the pop-up information. Click OK to move the conversation to Trash. 3. The conversation will remain in Trash for 30 days before being permanently deleted. If you want to permanently delete it sooner, follow the steps below to empty the Trash.

Bulk delete multiple conversations Delete one or more conversations at a time

  1. You can bulk-delete conversations in the My Conversations** and All Conversations views. Hover over the checkbox next to a conversation, select one or more conversations, then click Delete**.
  2. Carefully review the pop-up information. Click OK to move the conversation(s) to Trash.
  3. The conversation(s) will remain in Trash for 30 days before being permanently deleted. If you want to permanently delete it sooner, follow the steps below to empty the Trash.

Permanently delete conversations from the Trash Conversations will be automatically deleted from the Trash after 30 days, or delete them immediately

Conversations will be permanently deleted 30 days after they’re moved to the Trash folder. The time remaining until deletion is displayed on the right. Follow these steps to delete all conversations from the Trash folder immediately. 1. Go to the Trash folder and click Empty Trash Now. 2. Carefully review the pop-up. Click Delete Forever to permanently delete all conversations. 3. All conversations in the Trash folder have been permanently deleted and cannot be recovered.

Restore conversations Select converastions manually to delete or restore

You can also manually select individual conversations to restore or permanently delete from the Trash. #### Mobile App #### Delete a conversation on mobile Follow the instructions below to delete a conversation on the mobile Otter app. 1. Open the Otter mobile app. 2. You can quickly delete a conversation by long-pressing it and selecting Delete,

or by opening the conversation, tapping the three-dot menu in the upper-right corner, and selecting Delete. 3. Carefully review the delete prompt. Tap Delete to move the conversation to Trash. 4. The conversation will remain in Trash for 30 days before being permanently deleted. If you want to permanently delete it sooner, follow the steps below to delete conversations immediately.

Permanently delete conversations on mobile

Conversations will be permanently deleted 30 days after they’re moved to the Trash folder. The time remaining until deletion is displayed on the right. Follow these steps to delete all conversations from the Trash folder immediately. 1. Tap on Account in the lower right corner. Select Trash. 2. Tap Empty trash now or select individual conversations to permanently delete them. 3. Carefully review the prompt, then tap Delete to permanently delete all conversations from the Trash folder. 4. All conversations in the Trash folder have been permanently deleted and cannot be recovered.

FAQs

Can I recover a deleted conversation?

Deleted conversations can be restored from the Trash for up to 30 days after they’re deleted. Once a conversation is permanently deleted from the Trash or is automatically removed after 30 days, it can’t be restored or recovered. #### Does deleting a conversation credit those minutes to my account? No. Transcription minutes are counted when a conversation is recorded or transcribed. Deleting a conversation doesn’t restore or credit transcription minutes back to your monthly usage or billing cycle. #### Does the conversation stay on the server after deleting? No. Once a conversation is permanently deleted from the Trash, the conversation and its associated data are removed from Otter’s servers. #### Why can’t I delete a conversation that was shared with me? Only the conversation owner can delete a shared conversation. If a conversation has been shared with you and you no longer want to see it, contact the conversation owner and ask them to either: - Remove your access to the conversation, or - Delete the conversation if it’s no longer needed.

Why is the delete button grayed out?

If you select a conversation shared with you in All Conversations, the delete option will be grayed out. You can only delete conversations that you own.

Edit a conversation

You and anyCollaborators can edit a conversation to refine and improve its accuracy. Otter’s machine learning will learn from edits made to a conversation to improve the accuracy of future conversations. #### Editing a conversation Begin editing once the recording has stopped or the file has finished importing, and the audio is finished processing. ##### What can you edit in a conversation? - Add Takeaways - Correct the spelling of a word - Add or delete a word - Add, change, or delete punctuation - Insert or remove a paragraph break - Rename the conversation - Select or change the speaker

Editing text in a conversation on Web
Edit the conversation
  1. Sign in to your Otter account.
  2. Click on the conversation you want to edit.
  3. Click the Transcript tab and click Edit((image: pencil.png)) in the upper right corner to enter into editing mode. (image: edit a transcript.png)
  4. Begin editing the transcript.
  5. While editing, Otter will continuously save your edits within the current session.  (image: saving.png)

Note: Do not navigate away from this page during this time. You will still need to click Done to save all of your edits within your current editing session. 6. Once All changes saved status appears, click Done ((image: checkmark.png)) in the upper right corner to save your changes. (image: done editing.png) 7. Otter will begin to process the changes you made. Realigning text with audio will show in sections where Otter is attempting to realign the text to the audio. Once completed, the status will disappear indicating Otter has finished realigning the text. Wait until the status is gone before making further edits.  (image: realigning text with audio (large format).png)

Renaming the conversation 

While in a conversation, click on the title to edit. Click Enter/Return or the blue check () to save your edits. Tap anywhere in the conversation to begin editing. 5. Click Done to save your edits. (image: mobile_done_button.png)

Frequently Asked Questions (FAQs)

  • No. Editing and making corrections during a live conversation is not supported at this time. Conversations can only be edited once they’re finished processing.

  • You can add highlights, comments, and images, as well as interact with Otter Chat during a live recording of a conversation.

  • No. Trimming or cropping audio is not supported at this time.

  • Please avoid deleting large chunks of text within Otter itself, since Otter will always try to look for text to align to the spoken words inside the recording. As a workaround, please delete large chunks of text after exporting.

  • If you have large portions of the transcript you want to remove, you can export the audio and trim the audio in an editor. Once trimmed, you can import the file back into Otter to be transcribed.

Best practices
  • After each editing session, make sure to click Done ((image: checkmark.png)) before navigating to a different page, closing the browser window or tab, or closing the mobile Otter application.
  • Light edits to something that wasn’t actually said should be okay. Otter’s training process should be able to handle this without adversely affecting the quality of your future transcripts.
  • Please avoid deleting large chunks of text within Otter itself, since Otter will always try to look for text to align to the spoken words inside the recording. As a workaround, please delete large chunks of text after exporting.

Interact with your transcript

Enrich your conversations by adding reactions, comments, highlights, and images. These features facilitate deeper engagement by providing more context, emphasizing ideas, and offering further insights into your conversations. Collaborators can contribute meaningfully, fostering a more dynamic and productive exchange of ideas on the Conversation Page. Add reactions to comments, transcript content, and replies to enhance your conversations. Once a conversation has finished processing, hover over any section of text to bring up the toolbar. You have the option to quickly +1 () content or search through the vast available emojis to use by clicking the add emoji icon (). Add on to your team member’s reactions. You can hover over any reaction to see who else reacted with that emoji. Add, reply, and interact with comments to start conversations with your team members. #### Add a comment 1. In the Transcript tab of a conversation, highlight any content you want to leave a comment on to bring up the toolbar. 2. Click Comment (******) in the toolbar. 3. Type your comment and click the send button () on the right or hit Enter/Return on your keyboard. To notify another Otter user of your comment, tag them using @. 4. On the panel right of the conversation page, click the Comments** tab to view all comment threads you or your team members created.

Note: Comments cannot be sorted or reordered at this time.

Reply to comments

In the Comments tab, click into the Reply field to reply to any comment threads. Type your response and click the send button () on the right or hit Enter/Return on your keyboard to reply. To notify another Otter user of your comment, tag them using** @**. You can react to comments using emojis. #### Create a link or delete a comment You can generate a link to the comment  or delete a comment anytime by clicking the 3-dots () next to a comment: - Copy Link: Generates a direct link to the comment.

Note: Any users you share the link to will need collaborator permissions to view the comment. - Delete: Permanently deletes a comment. Deleted comments are not recoverable.

Highlight content during the live recording or post-recording to call out parts of the conversation you want to revisit or to remember important information. Select the content you want to highlight. Click highlight (). To quickly highlight, click highlight () on the controls panel and Otter will automatically highlight the current portion of the transcript either during playback or the most recently transcribed section during the live recording. View only your highlights View all of your highlights in one central location. On the Transcript tab, toggle on Show only highlights to view all highlights. You can playback and review just the highlights of your transcript. Add images to your conversations during the live recording or post-recording. 1. Highlight the text you want to add an image to and click Add image (******)** in the toolbar or control panel. 2. You have the option to upload from your device or paste from your clipboard. 3. Select the file you want to add. Click Open. (image: addimage.gif) 4. The image will be inserted next to the text.

Provide feedback to Otter

Feedback is essential to Otter’s growth and the improvement of our service. Whether it’s a suggestion, your experience, or a compliment, every piece of feedback is valued and plays a key role in shaping the future of our product. Your insights help us understand what’s working and what needs improvement, allowing us to continuously evolve Otter. - Product feedback settings - Collaborators providing feedback on shared conversations - Rate your conversation transcripts - Otter Chat feedback - Meeting title feedback - Overviews feedback - Action Items feedback - Outline feedback - Insights feedback - FAQs

Product feedback settings

Manage your feedback and training permissions in Account Settings > Meetings. You have the option to allow Otter and its third-party service provider(s) permission to access your conversations for training and product improvement, including through human review. See our Privacy Policy for more details, and our Privacy & Security page to learn how we keep your information private and secure. Otter is committed to the importance of security, trust, and transparency. 1. Navigate to Account Settings > Meetings. 2. Scroll down to Feedback and Training. 3. Next to Help improve Otter, set your preference in the drop-down.

Allow sharing

Allow sharing will allow Otter and its third-party service provider(s) permission to access all of your conversations in your account for training and product improvement, including human review. If your setting is set to anything other than Allow sharing, when submitting feedback, you’ll see the option to checkmark the “Allow Otter…” setting on the pop-up. This will take effect immediately and update your account settings in Account Settings > Meetings > Feedback and Training > Help improve Otter. ##### Don’t allow sharing If your setting is set to Don’t allow sharing, when submitting feedback, Otter will not access your conversation for training and product improvement. #### Collaborators providing feedback on shared conversations For shared conversations, - Collaborators have the option to provide feedback on a conversation. - Viewers are not able to provide feedback on a conversation.

If your account-level settings have not been set (shows Select an option), collaborators will be able to opt to allow Otter access to that conversation for training and product improvement on the pop-up. Once you set your account-level setting, they will no longer see that option. #### Rate your conversation transcripts 1. Navigate to the conversation you want to submit feedback on. 2. Click on the Transcript tab. 3. Scroll to the bottom of the transcript. Select your star rating. 4. Provide additional written feedback. Click Submit feedback.

Otter Chat feedback

Provide feedback on the answers provided by Otter AI Chat. Hover any answer to select Helpful or Not helpful. Add more details to submit your feedback. Your feedback is valuable and will help us continue to improve Otter AI Chat outputs. #### Meeting title feedback For meetings that are recorded ad hoc and not associated with a calendar event or an imported file, Otter will automatically generate a title for your conversations. Provide feedback to help improve the automatically generated titles for conversations. Click the 3-dot menu next to the title and select the feedback. Add your feedback and click Submit feedback. #### Overviews feedback Otter automatically generates an overview of your conversations for easy review. Click the 3-dot menu next to Overview and select the feedback. Add your feedback and click Submit feedback. #### Action items feedback Hover any Action Items to select Helpful or Not helpful for that item. You can submit feedback through the Homepage Action Items tab or on the Summary tab of a conversation. Add your feedback and click Submit feedback. #### Outline feedback Review the automatically generated outline for your conversations. Click the 3-dot menu to submit feedback for the entire section or hover over individual outlined items to submit feedback. Add your feedback and click Submit feedback. #### Insights feedback Otter can generate key points and insights based on the template you set for the conversation. Click the 3-dot menu and select the feedback. Add your feedback and click Submit feedback. #### FAQs #### What happens to past conversations I’ve previously given access to if I update to “Don’t allow sharing”? - Access to all past conversations will be removed for training. - Future conversations will not be used for training. - Update your settings anytime in Account Settings > Meetings > Feedback and Training.

Does “Allow sharing” give access to all my conversations, regardless of whether I submitted feedback for a conversation?

  • Yes.
  • Selecting Allow sharing will provide Otter access to all conversations in your account for training and product improvement.
  • You can manage and update that setting anytime in Account Settings > Meetings > Feedback and Training.
  • See our Privacy Policy for more details, and our Privacy & Security page to learn how we keep your information private and secure. Otter is committed to the importance of security, trust, and transparency.

I don’t see the option to update the permission setting on the feedback pop-up. Where can I find it?

  • If you previously checked the option to allow permissions to access your conversations for training, it will no longer show on the pop-up.
  • Checking the allow permissions will update your feedback and training session for the account to “Allow sharing”. You can manage and update that setting anytime in AccountSettings > Meetings > Feedback and Training.

Does undoing or removing my feedback selection change the account-level setting?

  • If you have previously submitted feedback, undoing or removing your feedback will not change the account-level setting if it was already set.
  • Manage and update that setting anytime in Account Settings > Meetings > Feedback and Training if you need to change your account level settings.

Can I provide or revoke access permission for just one conversation?

  • No.
  • Setting the option in Account Settings > Meetings > Feedback and Training will affect all of your conversations.

If a conversation has collaborators, are they able to submit feedback on conversations I share with them?

  • Yes.
  • Collaborators can submit feedback on conversations you have shared with them.
  • If you haven’t set your account level settings, collaborators with whom you have shared the conversation can submit feedback and opt to allow Otter access to those conversations for training and product improvement.

Note: When a collaborator checks that option, it will only affect that single conversation and not affect your account-level settings. - If your account-level settings are set to Don’t allow sharing, collaborators will be able to submit feedback, but not see the option to allow Otter to access the conversation for training and product improvement. - If your account-level settings are set to Allow sharing, collaborators will be able to submit feedback. Feedback submitted will allow Otter to access the conversation for training and product improvement.

Can I undo feedback?

  • Yes.
  • You can click Undo feedback or deselect the Helpful or Unhelpful state for any previously submitted feedback.

Why is the option to submit feedback missing for my account?

  • There may be various reasons why you may not see the submit feedback option for a conversation.
  • Check to see if you are a Viewer of a shared conversation. Viewers are not able to submit feedback.
  • If you are a member of a Workspace, reach out to your admin, or have them contact our Otter Support team for additional assistance.

React using emojis 👋

Add reactions to comments, transcript content, and replies to enhance your conversations. #### React with an emoji Once a conversation has finished processing, hover over any section of text to bring up the toolbar. You have the option to quickly +1 () content or search through the vast available emojis to use by clicking the add emoji icon (). Add on to your team member’s reactions. You can hover over any reaction to see who else reacted with that emoji.

Regenerate the summary

Easily update and refine the summary after editing the transcript. As you make changes through editing and tagging a speaker, keep it accurate and aligned with your latest revisions through our regenerate summary feature. The Gist, Action Items, and Outline summary will all be updated. #### Regenerate summary 1. Navigate to the conversation. 2. Update the conversation by editing the transcript or tagging speakers.

Tip: Click Update on the pop-up to immediately regenerate the summary after updating a conversation.  (image: done editing2 1.png) 3. Once you have finished updating the transcript, click on the 3-dot menu in the upper right corner. 4. Select Regenerate summary. 5. The summary will begin regenerating. 6. Once finished, the Gist, Action Items, and Outline summary will be regenerated.

Note: If any of the elements are missing, please refresh the page.

FAQs

Can I revert back to a previous summary version if I don’t like the new regenerated summary?

  • No.
  • At this time, previous versions are not saved. Once you regenerate a summary, there is no way to revert back to a previous version.
  • You can continue to update the conversation and regenerate the summary for other versions, or use Otter Chat separately to generate a summary.

Can I set it to automatically generate the summary if I edit the transcript or tag speakers?

  • No.
  • At this time, you will need to click Regenerate summaryeach time you want to update the summary with your new changes.

Can I regenerate the summary for multiple conversations in bulk?

  • No.
  • You will need to regenerate the summary individually for each conversation.

Can users with collaborator permission regenerate the summary for shared conversation?

  • Yes.
  • Users that have collaborator permission can regenerate the summary in a shared conversation.

Can I manually edit the summary Gist?

  • No.
  • At this time, the summary Gist cannot be manually edited.
  • Edit and update the transcript, and then regenerate the summary to update the summary Gist.

Can I manually edit the summary Action Items?

  • Yes.
  • You can edit and add action items.
  • Regenerating the summary will also update the summary Action Items.

Can I manually edit the summary Outline?

  • Yes.
  • You can manually edit the summary Outline.
  • Regenerating the summary will also update the summary Outline.

Rematch a speaker

If you have past conversations where you have not tagged the speakers yet and they still show as Speaker # (e.g., Speaker 1, Speaker 2, Speaker 3, etc.), but you know the speaker was tagged in a new conversation, you can rematch past conversations to tag those speakers with the new speaker data. Learn more about speaker identification. 1. Navigate to a conversation where you have a Speaker #.  2. Click on the 3-dot menu in the upper right corner. 3. Select Rematch. (image: rematch speaker 2.png) 4. Otter will use your account or Workspace’s present-day speaker data to tag any Speaker #s with a matched speaker.

Note: This is only for speakers that show as speaker #. Otter will not be able to rematch unknown speakers. 

Example:

  • Deborah records a meeting on February 1st, 2024. Deborah, Dani, and Gustavo spoke in the meeting. After the conversation finished processing, she noticed Otter was able to automatically tag the speaker Deborah, but not Dani and Gustavo, as it was the first time she had recorded a conversation with them.
  • Otter tags them (what would be Dani and Gustavo) as Speaker 1 and Speaker 2 respectively. Deborah decides to not tag them with names at that time.
  • Deborah later records a meeting on March 5th, 2024 with Dani and Gustavo again. This time she tags them as Dani and Gustavo.
  • Now that Dani and Gustavo have a speaker identification profile, Deborah goes back to the February 1st conversation and clicks Rematch.
  • Otter tags Speaker 1 and Speaker 2 as Dani and Gustavo respectively due to the new speaker identification data.

Remove highlighted text

You can unhighlight text anytime. 1. In the transcript, navigate to the highlight you want to remove.  (image: text to unhighlight.png) 2. Click any portion of the highlighted text. 3. Click on the Unhighlight icon in the pop-up menu. (image: unhighlight.png) 4. Review the information in the pop-up. Click OK to proceed. 

Note: Removing a highlight is permanent and cannot be undone. You will need to re-highlight the text to add the highlight back. (image: confirm unhighlight.png) 5. The highlight has been removed. (image: highlight removed.png)

Rename a speaker

You can rename a speaker anytime and it will update that speaker’s tag across all conversations. Learn more about speaker identification. 1. Click on any speaker icon to open the speaker list menu. 2. Search and find the speaker you want to rename. 3. Click on the 3-dot menu next to the speaker. Select Rename. **** 4. Type the new speaker name and click the blue checkmark to confirm the changes. ****

Otter will rename that speaker across all conversations with the updated name.

Retag and update a speaker tag

Otter will do its best to accurately tag speakers; however, there may be cases where you need to retag a speaker. Retagging a speaker will improve speaker identification in future conversation recordings. Learn more about speaker identification. 1. Click on the speaker you want to retag. 2. You can retag your speaker in a variety of ways:

Click on a suggested speaker 3. Search for an existing speaker 4. Create a new speaker 5. Click on the new speaker to confirm. 6. Wait for Otter to finish confirming the new speaker.

Speaker Identification Overview

Otter will automatically tag a speaker based on the speaker identification data in your account, as well as group paragraphs of the same speakers. The paragraph grouping is automatic. By tagging a speaker, you will teach Otter to recognize that speaker and improve upon the speaker identification for your future conversations, leading to less tagging in the future. Learn how to tag a speaker in your conversation. - Shared speakers - Automatically tagged speakers - New speakers - Unknown speakers - Manually tagged speakers - FAQS

Shared speakers

For users part of a Workspace, the member’s speaker profile can be automatically used by Otter to tag them in a conversation when you have a meeting with them. For users that have shared a conversation with users in a Channel, future conversations in that Channel can utilize the speaker tags within that Channel. Example 3: Workspace members’ speaker identification profiles are shared.  A Workspace has the members Soraya, Armin, and Kaori. A 4th member, Luna, was added later to the Workspace. Each of their speaker tags for their own profile is shared across the Workspace. Soraya starts a meeting with Armin. In Soraya’s conversation, Armin is automatically tagged, since he is in the same Workspace as Soraya, based on his speaker tag data. Example 2: A Channel member records a conversation where a shared speaker is recognized Bob belongs to the “Sales Team” Channel and adds “Jenny” as a speaker to a conversation that is shared with the “Sales Team” Channel. Another member of the “Sales Team” Channel records a conversation where “Jenny” is speaking. Otter will automatically recognize “Jenny” as a speaker and tag her name to the sections of the conversation where she spoke.

Example 3: A non-Channel member records a conversation with the same speaker Alice does not belong to the “Sales Team” Channel and records a conversation where “Jenny” is speaking. Otter will not add her name to the sections of the conversations where she spoke. Alice will need to tag “Jenny”. #### Automatically tagged speakers Otter uses the existing speakers you have in your account and shared speakers in your Workspace to automatically identify speakers in a conversation. Once a conversation has finished processing, view the Transcript tab to see the identified speakers. #### New speakers For conversations where a speaker has never been tagged before, Otter will tag them as Speaker # (i.e., Speaker 1, Speaker 2, Speaker 3, etc.). This indicates that Otter was able to distinguish a unique speaker throughout the conversation, but not associated with any current identified speaker in your Otter account. Tagging these speakers will automatically tag all other associated Speaker #s in the conversation. #### Unknown speakers If Otter was not able to identify a unique speaker, it may show as an unknown speaker . You can tag these speakers to start building the speaker identification for that speaker. #### Manually tagged speakers You can manually tag speakers to help Otter learn the speaker’s voices and identification. Each time you tag a speaker, Otter will be able to recognize that speaker in future conversations, so you have less tagging to do in the future. A green check mark will appear next to speakers to indicate that the speaker was manually tagged. #### FAQs - No. You cannot delete a speaker at this time. - Our team is working on adding speaker management in the future. - In the meantime, if you have any speaker management issues, please contact our Otter Support Team.

  • No. The Rematch feature will only tag the speaker identified as a Speaker # (e.g. Speaker 1, Speaker 2, Speaker 3, etc.)

  • You will need to manually retag existing tagged speakers.

  • No. The Rematch feature will only tag the speaker identified as a Speaker # (e.g. Speaker 1, Speaker 2, Speaker 3, etc.).

  • You will need to manually tag the unknown speakers.

  • No. You can only begin tagging a speaker after the recording has stopped.

Speaker Management

Speaker management allows you to tag speakers, merge duplicate speakers, and rename speakers across your transcripts, helping teams create cleaner, more accurate meeting notes and AI-generated Insights. Manage speakers in Otter to improve transcript accuracy, organize your speakers, and keep meeting conversations easy to review. #### Manage speakers Manage and organize speakers directly from the speaker profile avatar in your transcript. You can see which speakers you own, merge duplicate speakers, rename speakers, and hide speakers from the Manage Speakers menu. Speakers show how they were identified, whether they were manually tagged by you or automatically tagged by a meeting platform, such as Zoom or Google Meet. #### Merge speakers Combine duplicate or multiple speakers Multiple conversations may contain different speaker tags for the same person due to manual tagging, previously assigned speaker labels, or differences in how a person sounds across platforms (e.g., Zoom vs. in-person). You can merge speakers to maintain consistent speaker identification across your conversations and improve transcript accuracy. Merged speakers will update and reflect across all of your conversations. 1. Under Speakers, select the speaker(s) you want to merge. Then, in the Choose a speaker drop-down, select the speaker profile you want the selected speakers to merge into. 2. Click Merge, then select Yes, merge to confirm the speaker merge. 3. The selected speakers are successfully merged.

All conversations in your Otter account will reflect the merged speakers. ##### Primary speaker When you create an Otter account, a primary speaker profile is automatically created and linked to your account. This primary speaker profile represents your default speaker identity in conversations. Your primary speaker profile will not appear in the Manage Speakers list as a speaker that can be merged into other profiles. However, you may merge other speaker profiles into your primary speaker profile. Your primary speaker profile can be selected as an option to merge into. Your primary speaker profile is identified by your Otter account information. #### Unmerge speakers Undo a speaker merge at any time You can undo a speaker merge at any time to restore previously merged speaker profiles. Any restored speaker profile will be reflected across your conversations. 1. Click the 3-dot menu next to the speaker you want to unmerge, select Undo merge, and click Unmerge to confirm. 2. The speakers are successfully unmerged.

All conversations in your Otter account will reflect the unmerged speakers. #### Tag a speaker Tag or update speakers in a transcript Whether you are updating an existing speaker tag, tagging an identified speaker (such as Speaker 1, Speaker 2, or Speaker #), or assigning a tag to an untagged speaker bubble, click any speaker profile bubble and select a speaker to tag. You can choose an existing speaker profile or create a new speaker. To update the summary after tagging, click Update in the pop-up menu or Regenerate Summary anytime in the 3-dot menu. #### Rename a speaker Rename existing speakers in your transcripts You can rename a speaker at any time to improve speaker identification and maintain accurate transcript organization across your conversations. 1. Click any speaker profile bubble and select Manage Speakers to open the speaker management menu. 2. Click the Rename icon next to the speaker you want to rename. 3. Enter the new speaker name, then click the checkmark to confirm the update. 4. The updated speaker name will automatically apply across all conversations in your Otter account.

Hide a speaker Permanently hide speakers as an option for tagging

You can permanently hide a speaker from your speaker list. Existing tags of that speaker will not be affected. 1. Click any speaker profile bubble and select Manage Speakers to open the speaker management menu. 2. Click the Hide speaker icon next to the speaker you want to remove, then click Hide to confirm. 3. When you hide a speaker: In new conversations, or conversations where the speaker hasn’t already been tagged, the speaker won’t appear as a tagging option. 4. The speaker is removed from the Manage Speakers list. 5. Existing speaker tags remain unchanged and can still be untagged. 6. Previously tagged conversations continue to display the speaker as a grayed-out option in the tagging menu.

FAQs

Can I unhide a speaker?

No. Hiding a speaker is permanent and cannot be undone. Once a speaker is hidden, their profile is permanently removed from the tagging menu. #### How do I choose which speaker to merge into? When merging speakers, choose the speaker profile that represents the primary or canonical speaker identity. In the Manage Speakers panel, speaker profiles may include labels such as: - Tagged by you — A speaker profile created from manually tagging transcript segments - Your Otter account — Your primary speaker profile linked to your Otter account

In most cases, we recommend merging confirmed duplicate speakers into your primary account-linked speaker profile if they match. Confirmed duplicate speakers should ensure the speaker voices match (not just the name). This can help maintain consistent speaker identification across all conversations. #### Do speaker merges update all past and future conversations? Yes. Speaker merges and speaker renames apply across all conversations in your Otter account, including past and future transcripts. This helps maintain consistent speaker identification and speaker names across meetings and conversations. #### Why are some speakers missing from my Manage Speakers list? The Manage Speakers list only shows speakers that you own or created in your Otter account. Some speakers may not appear because they are: - Shared workspace speakers - Speakers tagged by other Otter users in a shared conversation - Primary speaker profiles associated with other Otter accounts

These speakers may still appear in the speaker tagging menu, but they cannot be managed from your Manage Speakers list. #### Which speakers can I manage? The Manage Speakers panel only displays speakers that you own, such as speakers you created by tagging or speaker profiles associated with your Otter account. The speaker tagging menu may display additional speakers because it can also include: - Shared workspace speakers - Speakers tagged by other Otter users in shared conversations - Primary speaker profiles associated with other Otter accounts

Only speakers you created and own will appear in your Manage Speakers list and can be managed, renamed, merged, or hidden. If a speaker does not appear in the list, ensure you are the original owner of that speaker profile. #### How do I rename my primary speaker profile? You can rename your primary speaker profile by updating your name in Account Settings → General.

Tagging speaker names in a conversation

Tag speakers in a conversation to distinguish who is speaking and train Otter to automatically identify speakers in future conversations. #### Speaker Identification Otter will automatically tag a speaker if it can, as well as group paragraphs of the same speakers. The paragraph grouping is automatic. By tagging a speaker, you will teach Otter to recognize that speaker and improve upon the speaker identification for your future conversations, leading to less tagging in the future. - Shared speakers - Automatically tagged speakers - New speakers - Unknown speakers - Manually tagged speakers

Shared speakers

For users part of a Workspace, the member’s speaker profile can be automatically used by Otter to tag them in a conversation when you have a meeting with them. For users that have shared a conversation with users in a Channel, future conversations in that Channel can utilize the speaker tags within that Channel. Example 3: Workspace members’ speaker identification profiles are shared.  A Workspace has the members Soraya, Armin, and Kaori. A 4th member, Luna, was added later to the Workspace. Each of their speaker tags for their own profile is shared across the Workspace. Soraya starts a meeting with Armin. In Soraya’s conversation, Armin is automatically tagged, since he is in the same Workspace as Soraya, based on his speaker tag data. Example 2: A Channel member records a conversation where a shared speaker is recognized

Bob belongs to the “Sales Team” Channel and adds “Jenny” as a speaker to a conversation that is shared with the “Sales Team” Channel. Another member of the “Sales Team” Channel records a conversation where “Jenny” is speaking. Otter will automatically recognize “Jenny” as a speaker and tag her name to the sections of the conversation where she spoke.

Example 3: A non-Channel member records a conversation with the same speaker Alice does not belong to the “Sales Team” Channel and records a conversation where “Jenny” is speaking. Otter will not add her name to the sections of the conversations where she spoke. Alice will need to tag “Jenny”. ##### Automatically tagged speakers Otter uses the existing speakers you have in your account and shared speakers in your Workspace to automatically identify speakers in a conversation. ##### New speakers For conversations where a speaker has never been tagged before, Otter will tag them as Speaker # (i.e., Speaker 1, Speaker 2, Speaker 3, etc.). This indicates that Otter was able to distinguish a unique speaker throughout the conversation, but not associated with any current identified speaker in your Otter account. Tagging these speakers will automatically tag all other associated Speaker #s in the conversation. ##### Unknown speakers If Otter was not able to identify a unique speaker, it may show as an unknown speaker . You can tag these speakers to start building the speaker identification for that speaker. ##### Manually tagged speakers You can manually tag speakers to help Otter learn the speaker’s voices and identification. Each time you tag a speaker, Otter will be able to recognize that speaker in future conversations, so you have less tagging to do in the future. A green check mark will appear next to speakers to indicate that the speaker was manually tagged. #### Tag a speaker You can tag a speaker to begin building the voice identification for that speaker. 1. Navigate to the conversation. 2. Click on the Transcript tab. 3. Click on the speaker icon (e.g., Speaker 1). A pop-up will appear with a list of suggested speakers. You can tag your speaker in a variety of ways: Click on a suggested speaker 4. Search for an existing speaker 5. Create a new speaker.

  1. Otter will begin tagging and confirming the new speaker tag. All speakers with the same identified # will be tagged as that speaker. Wait until the speaker has completed.
  2. You will see a green checkmark next to the speaker once the speaker tagging process has been completed.

Otter will do its best to accurately tag speakers; however, the may be cases where you need to retag a speaker. 1. Click on the speaker you want to retag. 2. You can retag your speaker in a variety of ways: Click on a suggested speaker 3. Search for an existing speaker 4. Create a new speaker

  1. Click on the new speaker to confirm.
  2. Wait for Otter to finish confirming the new speaker.

You can rename a speaker anytime and it will update that speaker’s tag across all conversations. 1. Click on any speaker icon to open the speaker list menu. 2. Search and find the speaker you want to rename. 3. Click on the 3-dot menu next to the speaker. Select Rename. **** 4. Type the new speaker name and click the blue checkmark to confirm the changes.  ****

If you have past conversations where you have not tagged the speakers yet and they still show as Speaker # (e.g., Speaker 1, Speaker 2, Speaker 3, etc.), but you know the speaker was tagged in a new conversation, you can rematch past conversations to tag those speakers with the new speaker data. Otter will use the recent most up-to-date speaker identification data in your account or Workspace to rematch those speakers. 1. Navigate to a conversation where you have a Speaker #.  2. Click on the 3-dot menu in the upper right corner. 3. Select Rematch.  (image: rematch speaker 2.png) 4. Otter will use your account or Workspace’s present-day speaker data to tag any Speaker #s with a matched speaker.

Note: This is only for speakers that show as speaker #. Otter will not be able to rematch unknown speakers. 

Example: - Deborah records a meeting on February 1st, 2024. Deborah, Dani, and Gustavo spoke in the meeting. After the conversation finished processing, she noticed Otter was able to automatically tag the speaker Deborah, but not Dani and Gustavo, as it was the first time she had recorded a conversation with them. - Otter tags them (what would be Dani and Gustavo) as Speaker 1 and Speaker 2 respectively. Deborah decides to not tag them with names at that time. - Deborah later records a meeting on March 5th, 2024 with Dani and Gustavo again. This time she tags them as Dani and Gustavo. - Now that Dani and Gustavo have a speaker identification profile, Deborah goes back to the February 1st conversation and clicks Rematch. - Otter tags Speaker 1 and Speaker 2 as Dani and Gustavo respectively due to the new speaker identification data.

You can untag a speaker anytime to disassociate that audio from that speaker. We recommend retagging the speaker instead of untagging it so that Otter can associate the correct speaker with the audio. 1. Click on the speaker you want to untag. 2. Click Untag. **** 3. Otter will untag that speaker.

Note: Otter will only untag that paragraph the speaker is associated with. If there are places in the transcript where the speaker was tagged incorrectly, you will need to untag them there as well. If you continue to see incorrect tagging issues, please contact our Otter Support Team.

FAQs

  • No. You cannot delete a speaker at this time.

  • Our team is working on adding speaker management in the future.

  • In the meantime, if you have any speaker management issues, please contact our Otter Support Team.

  • No. The Rematch feature will only tag the speaker identified as a Speaker # (e.g. Speaker 1, Speaker 2, Speaker 3, etc.)

  • You will need to manually retag existing tagged speakers.

  • No. The Rematch feature will only tag the speaker identified as a Speaker # (e.g. Speaker 1, Speaker 2, Speaker 3, etc.).

  • You will need to manually tag the unknown speakers.

  • No. You can only begin tagging a speaker after the recording has stopped.

Untag a speaker

You can untag a speaker anytime to disassociate that audio from that speaker. We recommend retagging the speaker instead of untagging it so that Otter can associate the correct speaker with the audio. Learn more about speaker identification. 1. Click on the speaker you want to untag. 2. Click Untag. **** 3. Otter will untag that speaker.

Using Otter: Share conversations

Allow collaborators to share

As a conversation owner, you can allow Collaborators to share your conversation with additional people. This makes it easier to share and collaborate on notes with your team, without you having to manually share the conversation every time with new people. By default, Collaborators have permission to share conversations and configure sharing (such as changing permission levels, changing the link sharing settings, or revoking sharing). There are two ways you can manage the setting to allow collaborators to share: - Account-level setting: sets the default for new conversations you create - Per-conversation setting

Account level setting

This setting controls whether collaborators can share across all conversations you own. Changing this setting will affect all conversations you own where you have not manually changed the setting. 1. Click on your profile in the upper corner to expand the menu. Click on Account Settings. **** 2. Navigate to the Meetings tab. 3. You can manage the Allow Collaborators to share setting here. (image: account settings reshare.png)

Conversation setting

  1. Open a conversation you own.
  2. In the upper right corner, click on the Share button.
  3. Click on the gear icon in the lower right corner.
  4. You can check or uncheck the first checkbox Collaborators can share conversations. (image: reshare setting.png)
